### Epic North Star: North Star: Context-First Critique with Cumulative Finding Custody
Source: `.megaplan/initiatives/critique-ledger/NORTHSTAR.md`
Captured artifact: `anchors/north_star/epic.md`
Checksum: `35c76087dc3a70924f06c1446a882d8f28c4679db42d0fe266f5046b22662143`

```md
# North Star: Context-First Critique with Cumulative Finding Custody

## Destination

Every Megaplan critique loop preserves what every selected critic found, what
the evaluator concluded, what revision action followed, and why a finding is
open, resolved, rejected, deferred, duplicated, accepted as risk, or unknown.
Later rounds receive bounded domain context without erasing raw evidence or
pressuring critics to invent novelty.

The evaluator remains the semantic routing and reconciliation authority. WBC
and Megaplan custody mechanisms provide durable attempt/evidence boundaries;
the critique ledger never becomes an execution authority, lifecycle writer,
repair queue, or substitute for raw producer artifacts.

## Load-bearing invariants

1. Every critic attempt and parseable occurrence is immutable and addressable,
   including failed attempts and valid `no_additional_findings` results.
2. One cumulative logical finding set has bounded per-domain projections; a
   semantic merge never destroys or rewrites occurrences.
3. Each known finding has an explicit current disposition representing
   acted-on, ignored/non-action, deferred, rejected, duplicate, accepted-risk,
   unknown, or resolved semantics, with rationale and evidence limits.
4. Resolution and non-action include explicit reopen conditions. Relevant plan,
   evidence, contract, or repository changes make stale closure fail visible.
5. Semantic equivalence, refinement, regression, split, and reopen decisions
   are model judgments preserved as append-only reconciliation events.
6. Deterministic machinery proves custody, completeness, freshness,
   idempotency, schema/version compatibility, and exact input/output hashes.
7. The evaluator selects domain critics. Each history-aware pass receives all
   relevant findings—including non-blocking and cross-domain findings—within a
   declared budget and with explicit overflow behavior.
8. Blind discovery is optional; history-aware reconciliation is mandatory
   before revise/gate consumption.
9. `no_additional_findings`, `no_open_blocking_findings`, `no_known_findings`,
   and `no_adjacent_text_matches` remain distinct claims.
10. The reviser records one action or explicit non-action per requested finding;
    the gate sees complete cumulative truth and cannot infer absence from an
    omitted prompt row.
11. WBC owns supported-runtime attempt/effect evidence, durable payload
    references, receipts, persistence, and compatibility boundaries. The
    critique ledger owns immutable critic occurrences, semantic finding
    identities, disposition/reopen events, bounded history briefings, and
    rebuildable derived projections.
12. Existing Megaplan components retain critic selection, revision, gate, and
    lifecycle authority. The migration switches the complete critique loop at
    one coordinated cutover and then retires the replaced path; custody failure
    stops admission rather than falling back silently or declaring convergence.

## Success measures

- Zero dropped flagged producer outputs and 100% reconciliation coverage.
- The M6 blocked-handoff family reconstructs as one finding with five retained
  occurrences while the accepted replay limitation retains its disposition.
- At least 50% fewer duplicate revision actions than control.
- No more than a five-percentage-point loss in independent new-family recall.
- At least 95% of closure/non-action dispositions cite adequate evidence.
- Stale briefings, missing occurrences, invalid schemas, unsupported closures,
  and incomplete WBC custody fail closed before revise/gate behavior changes.
- Minimum cutover evidence is durable and attributable to exact source,
  implementation, corpus, schema, model, and profile revisions.
- After cutover there is one supported critique-ledger architecture, no active
  legacy consumption path, and projections rebuild from WBC-backed evidence.

## Anti-scope

- No embedding database, general semantic graph, project-memory platform, or
  hard-coded similarity threshold as semantic authority.
- No parallel execution ledger, authority plane, transition writer, repair
  queue, or lifecycle state.
- No silent truncation, historical backfill that invents semantic relations,
  automatic closure from wording similarity, or severity/disposition collapse.
- No canary, prolonged shadow authority, dual-write period, broad mixed-version
  support, per-boundary rollback, or rollout dashboard program.
- No deployment, restart, chain launch, or runtime mutation by these planning
  assets themselves.
```

        ### Task Summary
        Implement WBC-backed ledger persistence, import, and replay for the critique ledger by consuming the frozen CL1 contract through existing SqliteAttemptLedgerStore, SqliteLedgerOutbox, and SqliteLedgerPayloadStore seams. Append CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, FindingReconciliationEvent, and FindingDispositionEvent records atomically with DurableRef payload references; publish LedgerRevisionManifest only after complete occurrence accounting validates; build cumulative domain projections and deterministic read-only replay; and execute a one-time import from the retained M6 corpus (5 rounds, zero failures) into the CL1 schema target. The WBC substrate already satisfies the one-transaction atomicity requirement (SQLite WAL + BEGIN IMMEDIATE + shared connection), but two gaps must be bridged: critique domain records must route through existing generic AttemptEventType values via LedgerEvent.payload without extending the enum, and cross-attempt LedgerRevisionManifest aggregation must be implemented as a separate mapping layer since the store is attempt-scoped. The CL1 handoff is formally not accepted (accepted_for_cl2: false, 5 procedural blockers) but all must-gates pass and all material artifacts—schemas, corpus, oracle, semantic loop—are frozen with verifiable hashes. CL2 can proceed conditionally, tracking the 5 blockers as open gates.

        ### Key Evidence

The outbox's append_event_with_outbox already demonstrates atomic event+outbox writes in one BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction; the payload store shares the same store.conn and can participate in the same atomic boundary. Domain-bounded replay (b) and round-scoped replay (c) have NO existing index support—domain and round_label are critique-ledger concepts stored in CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, not in the WBC attempt_events table. Cumulative projection rebuild (d) requires a standalone sequence index since PRIMARY KEY groups by attempt_id first, preventing efficient cross-attempt ordered scans. Both a round_attempts mapping table (avoiding schema widening) and a standalone sequence index are achievable within the two-week scope. The CL1 constraint forbids widening the schema without a reviewed decision.         ### Test Expectations
        - [fail_to_pass] test_cl1_handoff: Validates accepted_for_cl2 derivation, schema hash freshness, and blocker inventory. Currently fails for CL2 consumption (accepted_for_cl2: false, 5 blockers). CL2 must track this as a conditional-acceptance gate—all must-gates pass, schemas are frozen, but formal acceptance is pending.
- [pass_to_pass] test_m6_corpus: Validates M6 corpus completeness, dual hashing, round coverage, artifact availability, and redaction correctness. Must pass—corpus is the import source.
- [pass_to_pass] test_semantic_loop: Validates deterministic replay: five occurrences reconcile to one semantic finding, replay twice produces identical hashes, zero-write assertion holds. Must pass—proves the replay contract CL2 must preserve when moving from in-memory to store-backed replay.
- [pass_to_pass] test_semantic_loop_failures: Validates closed-failure behavior: missing custody, invalid schema versions, corrupt evidence all fail cleanly without partial writes. CL2 must extend these to cover store-backed failure modes (PostTerminalAppendError, MonotonicSequenceError, persistence_failed).
- [pass_to_pass] test_policy_tables: Validates atomicity/failure table and privacy/retention table against landed WBC policy. Must pass—these tables define the persistence contract CL2 implements.
- [pass_to_pass] test_zero_write_mutation_gate: Validates semantic loop functions are pure—zero filesystem writes, zero subprocess calls. Must pass—CL2 replay must match this purity constraint (no model calls, no external effects).

        ### Constraints
        - Consume only the frozen CL1 handoff schemas and hashes—do not widen schema or ownership without a reviewed CL1 decision. This means routing critique domain records through existing AttemptEventType values (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, RECONCILIATION, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME) via LedgerEvent.payload, not extending the enum.
- All writes are idempotent, content-safe, access-controlled, and fault-tested. The 4 Step 4 invariants (monotonic sequence, idempotency-key dedup, exactly-one-terminal, post-terminal rejection) are already enforced by _append_tx—CL2 must not bypass them.
- Start persistence precedes critic dispatch; terminal persistence failure is visible persistence_failed|indeterminate, never clean success. The failure-atomicity-table governs all 13 failure modes.
- Occurrences are never merged away. Semantic identity and occurrence identity remain distinct. Projections are rebuildable and carry no positive authority.
- Historical gaps stay explicit; migration never invents duplicate, resolution, evidence, or reopen relationships. LegacyClassification enforces forward-fix-only.
- Replay issues no model call or external effect. The semantic loop's zero-write assertion (verified by test_zero_write_mutation_gate) must hold for store-backed replay.
- faults.json must remain writable by critique and gate phases during CL2. The coordinated cutover to read-only projection is CL5 scope. Custody artifacts are dual-written during CL2.
- No new database service, event bus, authority plane, generalized knowledge store, embeddings, broad historical backfill, dual-write window, or old-reader retirement before CL5.
- Every accepted producer occurrence appears once; duplicate replay is a no-op; partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete ledger revision. The idempotency-key dedup (checked before rejection) and post-terminal rejection enforce this.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Phase 1—Bridge critique domain to WBC event stream: Build a CritiqueLedgerSerializer that converts CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope → LedgerEvent (event_type=EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, payload=serialized envelope), FindingReconciliationEvent → LedgerEvent (event_type=RECONCILIATION, payload=serialized event), FindingDispositionEvent → LedgerEvent (event_type=EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME, payload=serialized event). Construct DurableRef instances for evidence payloads per the retention-class-mapping (occurrence envelopes: confidential/run; reconciliation/disposition: restricted/audit; manifests: restricted/audit). Wire through SqliteLedgerOutbox.append_event_with_outbox for atomic event+payload persistence. No enum extension needed—payload carries the typed critique record.

Phase 2—Implement occurrence append with start-persistence gate: Before critic dispatch, call _append_tx with a STARTED event. On terminal persistence failure, emit PERSISTENCE_FAILED (not clean success). Append each accepted occurrence as an EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT event with the serialized CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope in payload. Append reconciliation and disposition events after complete occurrence accounting validates. Only after all events for a revision are appended, construct and append a LedgerRevisionManifest event.

Phase 3—Cross-attempt LedgerRevisionManifest: Implement a revision_manifests table (revision_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, prior_revision_hash TEXT, input_set_hash TEXT, domain_completeness TEXT, wbc_receipt_refs TEXT, event_ids TEXT, created_at_ns INTEGER) in the same SQLite database. After all occurrences/reconciliations/dispositions for a revision are atomically appended, INSERT the manifest in the same transaction. This avoids widening attempt_events.

Phase 4—Replay indexes: Create a round_attempts mapping table (round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)) populated at occurrence-write time from CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label. Add CREATE INDEX idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence) for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans. Per-attempt replay uses existing PRIMARY KEY; domain/round replay joins round_attempts → attempt_events; cumulative rebuild scans idx_attempt_events_sequence.

Phase 5—One-time M6 import: Traverse m6-corpus.json rounds v1-v5. For each round: map critique_custody.findings[] to CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope instances, evaluator_verdict.flag_verifications[] to FindingReconciliationEvent/DispositionEvent instances, plan-level faults/contract to LedgerRevisionManifest inputs. Classify every legacy record through LegacyClassification: has_authoritative_source → BACKFILLED; otherwise UNKNOWN or UNRECONSTRUCTABLE. Apply the partial-parse contract from failure-atomicity-table even though the corpus has zero failures. Reject unsupported schema versions via SUPPORTED_VERSIONS gate before any write. Import into the WBC store using the same atomic append path—each imported occurrence is an immutable event. Mark all imported records with metadata.source='m6-corpus-one-time-import'.

Phase 6—Faults.json dual-write: Keep save_flag_registry writing to faults.json unchanged. Add a parallel WBC write: after update_flags_after_critique upserts FlagRecords into the registry, also emit a critique-ledger flag-sync event (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT with a FlagSyncEnvelope payload) into the WBC store. This dual-writes faults.json (for active readers during CL2) and the ledger (for eventual cutover to read-only projection in CL5).

Phase 7—Fixtures and negative tests: Add all required fixtures (current-source import, target-only, future-version, corrupt, redacted, unavailable, duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, crash). Prove negative tests: ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue, Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state. Prove duplicate replay is a no-op via idempotency-key dedup. Prove partial publication cannot expose incomplete revision (post-terminal rejection + atomic outbox).

### Open Questions
- [assume_and_proceed] The CL1 handoff has accepted_for_cl2: false with 5 procedural blockers (missing reviewer sign-off, M6 prerequisite INCOHERENT, proof-index FAILED, 4 ownership blockers, portfolio gate PC-SCOPE-BLOCKER-001). CL2 proceeds conditionally, tracking the 5 blockers as open gates tracked in the CL2 handoff, with the understanding that schema hashes must not change and any blocker resolution that alters schemas would require CL2 scope recalibration.)_
- [assume_and_proceed] The WBC AttemptEventType enum has no critique-specific event types (only 11 generic lifecycle types), but the CL1 constraint forbids widening the schema without a reviewed CL1 decision. How are CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, FindingReconciliationEvent, and FindingDispositionEvent represented in the WBC event stream? _(assumption: Critique domain records serialize through LedgerEvent.payload (which accepts arbitrary dict or DurableRef) using existing generic event types: EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT for CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, RECONCILIATION for FindingReconciliationEvent, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME for FindingDispositionEvent, and a LedgerRevisionManifest-specific event routed through EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT as well. The payload field carries the full typed record. This avoids enum extension entirely—the event_type signals the lifecycle role, the payload carries the domain-specific content. - [assume_and_proceed] The WBC attempt_events table is attempt-scoped with PRIMARY KEY (attempt_id, sequence). The LedgerRevisionManifest spans multiple attempts and requires cross-attempt aggregation. No cross-attempt table or aggregation mechanism currently exists. Can CL2 implement this within the two-week scope without widening attempt_events? _(assumption: Implement a separate revision_manifests table in the same SQLite database (revision_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, prior_revision_hash, input_set_hash, domain_completeness, wbc_receipt_refs, event_ids, created_at_ns). The table is populated atomically in the same transaction as the final event of a revision using the shared store.conn. This avoids widening attempt_events and keeps the domain-specific aggregation separate from the generic event stream—consistent with the locked decision that projections carry no positive authority.)_
- [assume_and_proceed] Domain-bounded and round-scoped replay have no existing index support in the WBC store—domain and round_label exist only in CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope (event_json), not as columns in attempt_events. The constraint forbids widening the attempt_events schema. What is the minimum viable approach within the two-week scope? _(assumption: Create a round_attempts mapping table (round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)) populated at occurrence-write time from CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label. Domain-bounded replay extends this pattern with a domain_attempts table if needed, or filters in memory after loading round-scoped events since domain cardinality is bounded (2-4 domains per briefing budget). Add CREATE INDEX idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence) for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans. This avoids widening attempt_events while enabling all four required replay patterns within the two-week scope.)_

        The engineering brief above is evidence gathered from the codebase. Treat it as the default working context, challenge its conclusions when the code disagrees, and only do targeted repository lookups when a concrete gap remains. Open questions in the brief are candidate concerns, not mandates — act on ones that materially affect the plan, dismiss immaterial or already-resolved ones with a brief reason, and treat question count as noise.

        Idea:
# CL2 — WBC-backed ledger persistence, import, and replay

## Outcome

Implement the CL1 record contract through existing WBC/plan-local persistence
seams, with atomic append, idempotent retry, rebuildable projections, read-only
replay, and one-time import from retained legacy artifacts. Leave CL3 one stable
target API and fixtures.

## In scope

- Persist critic start/result and immutable occurrence envelopes with WBC
  attempt/payload references and current raw/custody artifacts.
- Append reconciliation/disposition events and publish a ledger revision only
  after complete occurrence accounting validates.
- Build cumulative/domain projections and deterministic read-only replay.
- Add current-source import, target-only, future-version, corrupt, redacted,
  unavailable, duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, and crash fixtures.
- Add the target schema reader and explicit `legacy_unknown` import behavior;
  retain current flag/gate/finalize inputs only until the coordinated cutover.
- Implement freshness vectors and governed tombstone/unavailable handling.

## Out of scope

Evaluator selection, critic prompt changes, semantic auto-merge policy, reviser
or gate consumption, live cutover, old artifact deletion, or production
backfill of historical semantic relationships.

## Locked decisions

- Start persistence precedes critic dispatch; terminal persistence failure is
  visible `persistence_failed|indeterminate`, never clean success.
- Occurrences are never merged away. Semantic identity and occurrence identity
  remain distinct.
- Projections are rebuildable and carry no positive authority.
- Historical gaps stay explicit; migration never invents duplicate, resolution,
  evidence, or reopen relationships.
- WBC inline/reference, retention, redaction, ordering, and effect rules are
  reused, not reimplemented.

## Open questions

- Which landed backend satisfies one transaction, and where is durable
  prepare/outbox reconciliation required?
- What plan-local compatibility files remain necessary for old readers?
- How are partially parseable failed attempts admitted without promoting an
  invalid whole payload?
- What indexes are necessary within the two-week scope for domain/round replay?

## Constraints

Consume only an accepted CL1 handoff. Do not widen schema or ownership without a
new reviewed CL1 decision. All writes are idempotent, content-safe, access-
controlled, and fault-tested. Replay issues no model call or external effect.

## Done criteria

- Schema/golden, property, concurrency, crash/fault, compatibility, privacy,
  WBC ledger/evidence, and existing critique-custody suites pass.
- Every accepted producer occurrence appears once; duplicate replay is a no-op;
  partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete ledger revision.
- Projections rebuild byte-equivalently from retained inputs and surface
  missing/redacted evidence as unknown.
- The one-time importer preserves all available legacy evidence, marks semantic
  gaps unknown, and rejects unsupported target versions without partial publish.
- Negative tests prove ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue,
  Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state.

## Touchpoints

`arnold/workflow/execution_attempt_ledger.py`, `payload_policy.py`, boundary
evidence/compatibility/conformance; Megaplan critique custody/runtime, schemas,
artifact writers, flag registry readers, and focused WBC/critique tests.

## Anti-scope

No new database service, event bus, authority plane, generalized knowledge
store, embeddings, broad historical backfill, dual-write window, or old-reader
retirement before CL5.

## Written handoff to CL3

Write and review `docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json` with API/
schema hashes, backend/atomicity decision, one-time import map, replay hashes,
fixture index, freshness rules, fault results, backup/restore prerequisites, and
unresolved limits. CL3 must validate the handoff and replay fixtures before
constructing a briefing.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - Size each step so it can be implemented AND verified in a single worker turn (~15 min, one model conversation). A step that bundles "create the abstraction AND migrate every consumer AND write all the tests" is too large to finish in one turn and will fail (the worker runs out of time before it can implement, leaving the step blocked). For large mechanical refactors, split along the natural seam — one step to add the new abstraction + its unit tests, then one step per consumer to migrate (the last migration step also adds parity tests) — rather than one giant step. Higher complexity does not earn a step more time; it only routes to a stronger model for the same single turn.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.
        - Assign every plan step a complexity score 1–5 before finalize. Reason about complexity in the plan markdown (e.g. "Complexity: 2" per step), and surface it informally so the reviewer can audit:
          - 1 = trivial single-file mechanical change
          - 2 = simple change with tests to update
          - 3 = multi-file change with non-trivial logic
          - 4 = cross-cutting change with architecture implications
          - 5 = fundamental system change with high regression risk
        - Populate `changed_surfaces` with every concrete file path your plan will change or create. Include both source files and test files. Use repo-relative paths (e.g., `src/prompts.py`, `tests/test_prompts.py`). This list drives the deterministic test-selection blast radius; be complete. If your plan touches a file, list it.
        - (Optional) Populate `test_blast_radius` with your own scoped test-selection proposal. This complements the deterministic floor the system computes from `changed_surfaces`; you cannot narrow below the floor, but you can widen with additional selectors or escalate to full. If you intend a scoped finalize baseline while keeping the full suite as a hard gate, set `strategy` to "scoped", include concrete `selectors`, and set `full_suite_fallback` to true; the floor's full-suite requirement will be honored by the fallback without forcing the baseline strategy to "full".
        - When you include `test_blast_radius`, it MUST be schema-complete. Include every
field in this shape:
`{"strategy":"scoped","confidence":"high","selectors":[{"kind":"path","value":"tests/test_relevant.py","reason":"covers the changed surface"}],"changed_surfaces":["src/relevant.py"],"always_run":[],"full_suite_fallback":true,"rationale":"Why these tests cover the planned changes.","import_graph":{"degraded":false,"dependent_tests":0,"unresolved":[]}}`.
The runtime deterministically recomputes and merges the import graph and the
coverage floor from `changed_surfaces`; do not omit `confidence`, `always_run`,
`changed_surfaces`, or `import_graph` merely because those values are also
runtime-derived.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`src/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`src/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`src/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`src/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`src/handlers.py`, `src/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`src/handlers.py:400`, `src/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_validator.py`, `tests/test_integration.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_validator.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_integration.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

{"content": "     1|\"\"\"Execution attempt ledger schema for workflow boundary contracts.\n     2|\n     3|This module freezes the ``ExecutionAttemptLedger`` schema contract under\n     4|``arnold.workflow``. It defines the identity, ordering, provenance, and\n     5|append-position core of ordered attempt history for every supported runtime.\n     6|\n     7|Each attempt records:\n     8|\n     9|* Immutable workflow/run/graph-revision, step/boundary, invocation,\n    10|  attempt ordinal/id.\n    11|* Parent/causal lineage and actor/tool provenance.\n    12|* Runtime adapter identity and  16|* Idempotency key for each event.\n    17|* Occurred and observed timestamps (clocks alone never establish ordering).\n    18|\n    19|Required event types: started, completed, failed,    46|\n    47|.. _ledger-primitive-gaps:\n    48|\n    49|Current Primitive Gaps\n    50|----------------------\n    51|\n    52|The following gaps exist in the current ledger primitives and must be\n    53|addressed by future C2-C6 migration work. They are documented here so\n    54|consumers and reviewers do not mistake their absence for design intent.\n    55|\n    56|NDJSON Sequence Gaps\n    57|~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n    58|\n    59|The current ``LedgerPosition`` uses integer ``sequence`` and\n    60|``append_position`` fields. These are not backed by a real persistent\n    61|sequence generator. When the ledger is materialized to NDJSON storage\n    62|(one JSON object per line, appended), sequence gaps can arise from:\n    63|\n    64|1. **Append-without-flush**: A runtime writes an event to NDJSON but\n    65|   crashes before the fsync/close. The file may contain a truncated\n    66|   line or a partial write at EOF. The next restart sees the gap but\n    67|   has no durable record of whether the event was fully committed.\n    68|\n    69|2. **Concurrent append races**: If two runtime instances share the same\n    70|   NDJSON file without a file-level lock or append-index coordination,\n    71|   interleaved writes can produce unparseable lines and apparent\n    72|   sequence gaps.\n    73|\n    74|3. **Rotated/missing segment files**: If the NDJSON log is rotated\n    75|   (e.g., by logrotate or a retention job) without updating the\n    76|   ledger's segment manifest, events in the rotated segment appear\n    77|   missing, producing a sequence gap.\n    78|\n    79|**Mitigation path (C4-C5)**: Replace bare NDJSON appends with a\n    80|write-ahead log (WAL) or append-blob store that provides atomic\n    81|append-with-checksum, segment manifests, and gap-detection on open.\n    82|Until then, consumers MUST treat sequence gaps as ``indeterminate``\n    83|and MUST NOT silently skip them.\n    84|\n    85|Missing Transaction / Outbox Migration\n    86|~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n    87|\n    88|The North Star mandates that internal state/result publication uses\n    89|one transaction where the store permits, or a durable outbox /\n    90|prepare-commit protocol with deterministic reconciliation. The current\n    91|ledger schema has no representation for:\n    92|\n    93|1. **Transaction boundaries**: There is no ``transaction_begin`` /\n    94|   ``transaction_commit`` / ``transaction_abort`` event type. The\n    95|   ledger cannot express that a group of events were written atomically.\n    96|\n    97|2. **Outbox records**: There is no ``OutboxRecord`` type linking a\n    98|   ledger event to an outbox message that must be delivered to a\n    99|   downstream consumer. The outbox pattern (write event + outbox message\n   100|   in one transaction, then deliver from outbox) is not modeled.\n   101|\n   102|3. **Prepare-commit protocol**: There is no ``prepare`` / ``commit`` /\n   103|   ``rollback`` event vocabulary for two-phase commit across the ledger\n   104|   and an external store.\n   105|\n   106|**Mitigation path (C4-C6)**: Add ``transaction_begin``,\n   107|``transaction_commit``, and ``transaction_abort`` event types. Add an\n   108|``OutboxRecord`` dataclass with message identity, delivery target,\n   109|idempotency key, and delivery status. Add ``prepare`` and ``commit``\n   110|event types for two-phase scenarios. Until then, consumers MUST treat\n   111|every ledger event as independently durable and MUST NOT assume\n   112|atomicity across events.\n   113|\"\"\"\n   114|\n   115|from __future__ import {"content": "     1|\"\"\"Versioned, checksum-validated, crash-resumable schema migrations for the\n     2|M6A WBC transactional ledger substrate.\n     3|\n     4|This module provides the M6A migration boundary. It governs how the SQLite\n     5|schema that backs :class:`~arnold.workflow.attempt_ledger_store.SqliteAttemptLedgerStore`\n     6|and its sibling stores (outbox, payload) evolves over time, and how legacy or\n     7|unreconstructable records are classified.\n     8|\n     9|Design pillars (M6A success criterion 9):\n    10|* **Versioned** — every migration carries a strictly-increasing integer\n    11|  ``version`` and a stable ``name``. Migrations apply in ascending order and a\n    12|  gap in the applied sequence is never bridged silently.\n    13|* **Checksum-validated** — every migration's content (version + name +\n    14|  statements) is reduced to a deterministic SHA-256 checksum that is persisted\n    15|  alongside its applied-state record. On re-open the stored checksum is\n    16|  compared to the live registry's checksum; a mismatch is a forward-fix\n    17|  violation and is surfaced, never silently re-applied.\n    18|* **Crash-resumable** — each migration applies inside its *own* ``BEGIN\n    19|  IMMEDIATE`` transaction that atomically stages the schema change *and* the\n    20|  ``schema_migrations`` row. A crash mid-migration leaves either a fully\n    21|  applied+recorded migration or nothing — never a half-applied migration that a\n    22|  mixed-version reader could mistake for authoritative state. Resuming re-runs\n    23|  the unrecorded migration from scratch; DDL is idempotent (``IF NOT EXISTS``)\n    24|  so a partially-staged-but-uncommitted change is safely re-staged.\n    25|* **Forward-fix only** — there is no downgrade path. A migration, once applied,\n    26|  is immutable. To repair a defect you add a *new* migration; you never edit an\n    27|  applied one (the checksum guard enforces this). Unreconstructable legacy\n    28|  records are classified ``UNKNOWN`` / ``UNRECONSTRUCTABLE`` — success is never\n    29|  synthesized from logs, markers, or mutable state (SD2).\n    30|* **Idempotent on duplicate backfill** — ``migrate()`` is safe to call any\n    31|  number of times; already-applied migrations are skipped, not re-executed.\n    32|* **Mixed reader/writer safe** — migration state is a normal table read under\n    33|  WAL snapshot semantics, so a reader connection observes a consistent\n    34|  ``schema_migrations`` view (some prefix ``1..k`` applied) even while a writer\n    35|  is mid-migration.\n    36|\n    37|This module owns only migration-tracking and legacy-classification tables. {"content": "   190|class LegacyClassification(Enum):\n   191|    \"\"\"Classification of a legacy or unreconstructable record.\n   192|\n   193|    The key rule (SD2 / criterion 9): records that cannot be confidently\n   194|    reconstructed are ``UNKNOWN`` / ``UNRECONSTRUCTABLE``. No terminal/success outcome is synthesized.\n   201|    * ``CORRUPT`` — the record or its source evidence is structurally damaged.\n   202|    * ``UNRECONSTRUCTABLE`` — no source exists from which to reconstruct the\n   203|      record (e.g. the producing system never persisted it).\n   204|    \"\"\"\n   205|\n   206|    BACKFILLED = \"backfilled\"\n   207|    UNKNOWN = \"unknown\"\n   208|    CORRUPT = \"corrupt\"\n   209|    UNRECONSTRUCTABLE = \"unreconstructable\"\n   210|\n   211|\n   212|@dataclass(frozen=True)\n   213|class ClassificationEvidence:\n   214|    \"\"\"Evidence profile for a legacy record being classified.\n   215|\n   216|    This dataclass makes the evidence sources *explicit* so that the\n   217|    classification decision function can reject BACKFILLED when the authority\n   218|    derives from impermissible sources (logs, markers, receipts, mutable state).\n   219|\n   220|    Attributes:\n   221|        has_authoritative_source: The record can be reconstructed from\n   222|            authoritative, durable source data (e.g. a separate durable store\n   223|            with matching checksums). This is the ONLY path to BACKFILLED.\n   224|        has_logs: Operational logs mention the record. NOT authoritative.\n   225|        has_markers: Checkpoint/status markers reference the record. NOT\n   226|            authoritative.\n   227|        has_receipts: Delivery receipts or acknowledgments exist. NOT\n   228|            authoritative.\n   229|        has_mutable_state: A mutable system (e.g. in-memory cache, runtime\n   230|            state) holds data about the record. source_exists: ``False`` when no source system ever persisted the\n   234|            record (UNRECONSTRUCTABLE).\n   235|        notes: Free-form notes about the evidence assessment.\n   236| has_mutable_state: bool = False\n   243|    is_structurally_damaged: bool = False\n   244|    source_exists: bool = True\n   245|    notes: str = \"\"\n   246|\n   247|    @property\n   248|    def only_circumstantial(self) -> bool:\n   249|        \"\"\"True when evidence consists ONLY of impermissible sources\n   250|        (logs, markers, receipts, mutable state) with no authoritative source.\"\"\"\n   251|        return (\n   252|            not self.has_authoritative_source\n   253|            and (\n   254|                self.has_logs\n   255|                or self.has_markers\n   256|                or self.has_receipts\n   257|                or self.has_mutable_state\n   258|            )\n   259|        )\n   260|\n   261|    @property\n   262|    def no_evidence(self) -> bool:\n   263|        \"\"\"True when no evidence of any kind is available.\"\"\"\n   264|        return (\n   265|            not self.has_authoritative_source\n   266| Critique records must ride through `LedgerEvent.payload` using existing\\n   generic types (`EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT`, `RECONCILIATION`,\\n   `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME`). `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` requires a non-null\\n   `payload` or `payload_policy_ref` (`execution_attempt_ledger.py:837-842`).\\n2. **Attempt-scoped table**: `attempt_events` has no domain/round columns.\\n   Cross-attempt `LedgerRevisionManifest` aggregation and domain/round replay\\n   need separate tables in the same SQLite DB — not schema widening.\\n3. **Start precedes dispatch**: Terminal persistence failure is visible\\n   `persistence_failed|indeterminate`, never clean success\\n   (failure-atomicity-table modes 1–2, 13).\\n4. **Replay purity**: Store-backed replay issues no model call or external effect\\n   — it must match the in-memory `replay_full` zero-write assertion.\\n5. **faults.json stays writable**: CL2 does not change the flag write path;\\n   dual-write is a parallel ledger event, not a replacement.\\n6. **Idempotency**: Each critique record gets a stable idempotency key so\\n   duplicate replay is a no-op via the existing dedup check.\\n\\n### Anchor Tension (Flagged, Not Silently Resolved)\\n\\nThe CL1 handoff has `accepted_for_cl2: false` with 5 procedural blockers\\n(`cl1-contract-oracle.json:411-441`). The idea constraint says \\\"Consume only an\\naccepted CL1 handoff.\\\" However, all CL1 must-gates pass (custody, freshness,\\nreplay equality, authority, schema, prerequisite), all five schemas are frozen\\nwith verifiable SHA-256 hashes, and the 5 blockers are\\nprocedural/infrastructural (not material invalidations). The Epic North Star\\nmandates that \\\"custody failure stops admission rather than falling back\\nsilently\\\" and that cutover is \\\"one coordinated\\\" event.\\n\\n**Decision**: CL2 proceeds **conditionally**, treating the 5 blockers as tracked\\nopen gates validated by a dedicated test (Step 1). This is a **bridge**, not a\\nsilent override: schema hashes must not change, and any blocker resolution that\\nalters schemas would require CL2 scope recalibration. This tension is surfaced\\nexplicitly here and in the written handoff.\\n\\n## Main Phases\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 1: Precondition Gate + Serialization Foundation\\n\\n### Step 1: CL2 conditional-acceptance gate (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_handoff.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Small — **Complexity: 2**\\n\\n**Rationale:** Cheapest validation first. Confirms the frozen CL1 contract is\\nintact before building on it, and materializes the conditional-acceptance\\ndecision as a tracked gate.\\n\\n1. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_handoff.py` that loads\\n   `docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl1-contract-oracle.json` and asserts:\\n   - All CL1 must-gates pass (`accepted_for_cl2.checks.cl1_must_gates_pass.passed == true`).\\n   - All recorded file hashes are fresh (recompute from disk, compare to handoff\\n     `schema_and_version_hashes` and `manifest_oracle_gate_hashes` — reuse the\\n     `_sha256_file` / `HASHED_PATHS` pattern from `test_cl1_handoff.py:25-57`).\\n   - The 5 blocking reasons are inventoried and non-empty (they are open gates,\\n     not silent gaps).\\n   - `accepted_for_cl2.value == false` is the correct AND of the four checks\\n     (confirming the derivation, not asserting acceptance).\\n   - `SUPPORTED_VERSIONS == {\\\"cl.schema.v1\\\"}` is unchanged (no schema drift).\\n2. **Add** a constant `CL2_OPEN_GATES` listing the 5 blocker keys so CL3 can\\n   validate CL2 tracked them.\\n3. **Run** `pytest tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl1_handoff.py\\n   tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_handoff.py` to confirm both pass (the\\n   existing test validates derivation correctness; the new test validates CL2's\\n   conditional-acceptance posture).\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 2: CritiqueLedgerSerializer — pure CL1 ↔ LedgerEvent bridge (`arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_serializer.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Medium — **Complexity: 3**\\n\\n**Rationale:** The serializer is the single bridge point between frozen CL1\\ndomain records and the generic WBC event stream. It is pure (no I/O) so it fits\\nthe `arnold.critique_ledger` purity invariant and can be unit-tested in\\nisolation before any persistence wiring.\\n\\n1. **Create** `arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_serializer.py` with:\\n   - A `CRITIQUE_RECORD_TYPES` mapping: `\\\"occurrence\\\"` → `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT`,\\n     `\\\"reconciliation\\\"` → `RECONCILIATION`, `\\\"disposition\\\"` →\\n     `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME`, `\\\"manifest\\\"` → `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT`.\\n   - `CritiqueLedgerSerializer` with:\\n     - `envelope_to_event(occurrence, *, identity, provenance, adapter, versions, grant_ref, sequence, ...) -> LedgerEvent`: serializes the envelope dict\\n       into `payload` with an explicit discriminator wrapper:\\n       ```python\\n       payload = {\\n           \\\"critique_record_type\\\": \\\"occurrence\\\",\\n           \\\"schema_version\\\": \\\"cl.schema.v1\\\",\\n           \\\"record\\\": occurrence.to_dict(),\\n       }\\n       ```\\n       This satisfies the `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` payload requirement\\n       (`execution_attempt_ledger.py:837`) and makes deserialization\\n       unambiguous without overloading `event_type` semantics.\\n     - Same pattern for `reconciliation_to_event` and `disposition_to_event`.\\n     - `manifest_to_event(manifest, ...)` for `LedgerRevisionManifest`.\\n   - `CritiqueLedgerDeserializer` with:\\n     - `event_to_record(event: LedgerEvent) -> CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope | FindingReconciliationEvent | FindingDispositionEvent | LedgerRevisionManifest`:\\n       inspect `event.payload[\\\"critique_record_type\\\"]` and dispatch to the\\n       correct `from_dict`. Validate `schema_version` via `SUPPORTED_VERSIONS`\\n       before reconstruction (reject unsupported/future versions).\\n     - `extract_record_type(event) -> str | None`: returns the discriminator or\\n       `None` if the event is not a critique record (non-critique\\n       `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` events pass through untouched).\\n2. **Reject** any payload whose `schema_version` is not in `SUPPORTED_VERSIONS`\\n   — raise `ValueError` (forward-fix only; never preserve unknown versions).\\n3. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_ledger_serializer.py` with:\\n   - Round-trip tests: serialize each CL1 record type → `LedgerEvent` →\\n     deserialize → equals original (all four record types).\\n   - Discriminator tests: `extract_record_type` returns correct type; non-critique\\n     events return `None`.\\n   - Schema-version rejection: a payload with `\\\"cl.schema.v999\\\"` raises\\n     `ValueError`.\\n   - Idempotency-key stability: the same record always yields the same\\n     `idempotency_key` (derive from the record's identity field:\\n     `occurrence_id` / `reconciliation_id` / `disposition_id` / `manifest_id`).\\n4. **Export** `CritiqueLedgerSerializer`, `CritiqueLedgerDeserializer` from\\n   `arnold/critique_ledger/__init__.py`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 2: WBC-backed Persistence\\n\\n### Step 3: CritiqueLedgerStore — schema + occurrence append + start gate (`arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_store.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Large — **Complexity: 4**\\n\\n**Rationale:** The store is the WBC-backed persistence boundary for critique\\nrecords. It lives in `arnold.workflow` (I/O layer) and depends on both the WBC\\nstores and the pure `arnold.critique_ledger` schemas. This step adds the schema\\nand the core occurrence write path with the start-persistence gate.\\n\\n1. **Create** `arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_store.py` with:\\n   - **Schema DDL** (created on `store.conn`, same DB, idempotent `IF NOT EXISTS`):\\n     - `_CRITIQUE_REVISION_MANIFESTS_DDL`: `revision_manifests` table —\\n       `revision_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, prior_revision_hash TEXT,\\n       input_set_hash TEXT, domain_completeness TEXT (JSON),\\n       wbc_receipt_refs TEXT (JSON), event_ids TEXT (JSON), manifest_json TEXT,\\n       created_at_ns INTEGER`.\\n     - `_ROUND_ATTEMPTS_DDL`: `round_attempts` mapping table —\\n       `round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)`.\\n     - `_EVENTS_SEQUENCE_INDEX_DDL`:\\n       `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence)`\\n       for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans.\\n   - **`CritiqueLedgerStore`** class wrapping `SqliteAttemptLedgerStore` +\\n     `SqliteLedgerOutbox` + `SqliteLedgerPayloadStore` (all sharing `store.conn`):\\n     - `__init__(store, outbox, payload_store)`: store references, init schema.\\n     - `append_started(attempt_id, identity, ...) -> AppendResult`: append a\\n       `STARTED` `LedgerEvent` via `store._append_tx`. **This is the\\n       start-persistence gate** — must succeed before any occurrence is appended.\\n     - `append_occurrence(attempt_id, occurrence, *, identity, ...) -> AppendResult`:\\n       use `CritiqueLedgerSerializer.envelope_to_event` to build the\\n       `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` event, then call\\n       `outbox.append_event_with_outbox(attempt_id, event, outbox_payloads=[])`.\\n       If the occurrence evidence payload exceeds the inline threshold, route\\n       through `payload_store.store_payload` first and embed the resulting\\n       `DurableRef` in the event payload instead of the inline dict.\\n     - **Populate `round_attempts`**: in the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction\\n       as the occurrence append, `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO round_attempts` the\\n       `(occurrence.round_label, attempt_id)` pair. This requires a small\\n       extension to the append path — execute the round_attempts INSERT on the\\n       shared `store.conn` immediately after the event INSERT and before COMMIT.\\n       (The outbox method's transaction is open; we add the round_attempts write\\n       by providing a callback hook or a thin wrapper that runs the extra INSERT\\n       inside the same transaction.)\\n2. **Handle the transaction boundary**: Since `append_event_with_outbox` manages\\n   its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`/`COMMIT`, the round_attempts population needs to\\n   participate. Implement `append_occurrence` as a custom transaction method on\\n   `CritiqueLedgerStore` that mirrors the outbox's dedup → post-terminal →\\n   monotonic → INSERT event → INSERT round_attempts → COMMIT sequence, all on\\n   `store.conn`. Reuse `_begin_immediate_retry`.\\n3. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_critique_ledger_store.py` with:\\n   - Schema initialization test: tables and indexes exist after construction.\\n   - Start-gate test: `append_started` succeeds; a subsequent occurrence append\\n     works.\\n   - Start-gate failure: if the store is closed/corrupted, `append_started`\\n     raises and no occurrence is written.\\n   - Occurrence append: event lands in `attempt_events` with correct\\n     `event_type` and deserializable payload; `round_attempts` row inserted.\\n   - Idempotency: appending the same occurrence twice returns `is_duplicate=True`\\n     and does not create a second event.\\n   - Inline vs reference routing: small payload inline, large payload via\\n     `payload_store` with `DurableRef` in event.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 4: CritiqueLedgerStore — reconciliation/disposition append + manifest publication (`arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_store.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Medium — **Complexity: 3**\\n\\n1. **Add** to `CritiqueLedgerStore`:\\n   - `append_reconciliation(attempt_id, reconciliation, ...) -> AppendResult`:\\n     serialize via `reconciliation_to_event` (`RECONCILIATION` type), append via\\n     the same transactional path.\\n   - `append_disposition(attempt_id, disposition, ...) -> AppendResult`:\\n     serialize via `disposition_to_event` (`EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME` type),\\n     append via the same transactional path.\\n2. **Add** `publish_manifest(manifest: LedgerRevisionManifest) -> ManifestPublishResult`:\\n   - **Complete-occurrence validation**: Before INSERT, query `attempt_events`\\n     to confirm every `manifest.event_ids` occurrence is durably persisted and\\n     has a valid (parseable, non-FAILED) record. If any referenced occurrence is\\n     missing or invalid, raise `ManifestIncompleteError` (typed) and do NOT\\n     INSERT — this enforces \\\"partial publication cannot expose an accepted\\n     incomplete ledger revision.\\\"\\n   - **Atomic INSERT**: `INSERT INTO revision_manifests` with the manifest's\\n     `input_set_hash` as `revision_hash` (PK), the full manifest JSON, and all\\n     vector fields. Run in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.\\n   - **Idempotency**: If the `revision_hash` already exists, return the existing\\n     manifest (no-op) — duplicate replay is safe.\\n   - **Terminal persistence failure**: If the INSERT fails, the revision is not\\n     admitted as clean success. Surface the failure (failure-atomicity-table\\n     mode 13: `terminal_persistence_failure`).\\n3. **Add** `read_manifest(revision_hash: str) -> LedgerRevisionManifest | None`\\n   and `latest_manifest() -> LedgerRevisionManifest | None`.\\n4. **Extend** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_critique_ledger_store.py`:\\n   - Reconciliation/disposition append + round-trip via deserializer.\\n   - Manifest publication: succeeds when all occurrences present; fails when an\\n     occurrence is missing (assert `ManifestIncompleteError` and that no manifest\\n     row exists).\\n   - Manifest idempotency: publishing the same manifest twice is a no-op.\\n   - Sequence: start → occurrences → reconciliations → dispositions → manifest;\\n     manifest fails if any prior step is skipped.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 3: Store-backed Read-only Replay\\n\\n### Step 5: CritiqueLedgerReplay — deterministic store-backed replay (`arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_replay.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Large — **Complexity: 4**\\n\\n**Rationale:** The in-memory `replay_full` (`semantic_loop.py:964-1206`) is the\\ncontract CL2 must reproduce from the store. Store-backed replay reads persisted\\nevents, reconstructs typed records, and feeds them to the existing pure\\n`replay_full` — guaranteeing byte-equivalent projections without reimplementing\\nthe semantic loop.\\n\\n1. **Create** `arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_replay.py` with\\n   `CritiqueLedgerReplay`:\\n   - `replay_attempt(attempt_id) -> list[CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope | ...]`:\\n     read events via `store.read_events(attempt_id)`, filter to critique records\\n     via `Deserializer.extract_record_type`, reconstruct typed records.\\n   - `replay_round(round_label) -> dict[str, list]`:\\n     `SELECT attempt_id FROM round_attempts WHERE round_label = ?` → for each\\n     attempt, `replay_attempt` → collect occurrences/reconciliations/dispositions.\\n   - `replay_domain(domain, occurrence_ids) -> dict`:\\n     domain cardinality is bounded (2–4 per briefing budget); filter\\n     in-memory after loading round/all events (domain lives in the occurrence\\n     envelope, not a column).\\n   - `replay_cumulative() -> dict[str, list]`:\\n     `SELECT event_json FROM attempt_events ORDER BY sequence ASC` (uses\\n     `idx_attempt_events_sequence`) → reconstruct all critique records in global\\n     append order.\\n   - `replay_revision(revision_hash) -> dict`:\\n     load the `LedgerRevisionManifest`, load its `event_ids`, reconstruct those\\n     occurrences, then call `replay_full` to produce the full projection set\\n     (custody, reconciliation, disposition, manifest, briefing,\\n     reviser_projection, gate_projection).\\n2. **Purity guarantee**: `CritiqueLedgerReplay` performs only `SELECT` reads\\n   (no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE). It delegates to the pure `replay_full` for\\n   projection computation. No model calls, no subprocess, no filesystem writes.\\n   The existing `test_zero_write_mutation_gate` pattern must hold — add an\\n   equivalent assertion.\\n3. **Byte-equivalence**: `replay_revision` must produce projections identical to\\n   calling `replay_full` directly with the same in-memory lists. Assert by\\n   comparing `canonical_hash` of both projection dicts.\\n4. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_critique_ledger_replay.py`:\\n   - Round-trip: write occurrences/reconciliations/dispositions/manifest via\\n     `CritiqueLedgerStore`, then replay and assert byte-equivalence with\\n     direct `replay_full`.\\n   - Replay twice → identical hashes (determinism).\\n   - Per-attempt, round-scoped, cumulative replay all return consistent subsets.\\n   - Zero-write assertion: replay does not modify the store (row counts\\n     unchanged, no new files).\\n   - Replay with a missing manifest returns None / empty (no partial projection).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 4: One-time M6 Import\\n\\n### Step 6: M6 one-time importer (`arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_importer.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Large — **Complexity: 4**\\n\\n1. **Create** `arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_importer.py` with\\n   `M6CorpusImporter`:\\n   - `import_corpus(corpus_path, store) -> ImportReport`:\\n     - Load `m6-corpus.json`, validate `meta.raw_hash` and `meta.redacted_hash`\\n       against the file on disk.\\n     - **Schema gate**: before any write, assert\\n       `corpus[\\\"meta\\\"][\\\"schema_version\\\"] == \\\"cl.m6-corpus.v1\\\"` and the target\\n       `SUPPORTED_VERSIONS == {\\\"cl.schema.v1\\\"}`. Reject unsupported target\\n       versions with a typed error (failure-atomicity-table mode:\\n       `unsupported_schema_version`).\\n     - **Traverse rounds v1–v5**: for each round, map:\\n       - `round.critique_custody.findings[]` → `CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope`\\n         instances (occurrence_id, finding_id, producer_id, parse_status,\\n         evidence hashes from the corpus's dual-hashed fields).\\n       - `round.evaluator_verdict.flag_verifications[]` →\\n         `FindingReconciliationEvent` and `FindingDispositionEvent` instances\\n         (map verified/disputed/resolved to reconciliation relationships and\\n         disposition families).\\n       - Plan-level `faults`/`contract` → manifest `input_set_hash` inputs.\\n     - **Classify every legacy record** via `LegacyClassification`\\n       (`ledger_migrations.py:190-270`):\\n       - The M6 corpus is an authoritative source (frozen, hash-verified) →\\n         `ClassificationEvidence(has_authoritative_source=True)` →\\n         `BACKFILLED` for records that map cleanly.\\n       - Records that cannot map (missing field, ambiguous) → `UNKNOWN` (never\\n         `BACKFILLED` from circumstantial evidence —\\n         `ClassificationSafetyError` if attempted).\\n     - **Apply the partial-parse contract** even though the corpus has zero\\n       failures: validate each occurrence's `parse_status`; FAILED → record\\n       attempt, no findings; MALFORMED → `UNAVAILABLE` with reopen condition;\\n       TOMBSTONED → mark removed.\\n     - **Atomic import**: use `CritiqueLedgerStore.append_*` for each record —\\n       each imported occurrence is an immutable event. Mark all imported records\\n       with `metadata.source = \\\"m6-corpus-one-time-import\\\"`.\\n     - Return an `ImportReport` with counts (rounds, occurrences,\\n       reconciliations, dispositions, manifests, classifications, any gaps\\n       marked UNKNOWN).\\n2. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_m6_importer.py`:\\n   - Import the full 5-round corpus → assert all occurrences preserved, zero\\n     dropped, zero FAILED/DROPPED/MALFORMED (oracle fact 6).\\n   - Idempotency: importing twice is a no-op (idempotency-key dedup).\\n   - Future-version rejection: a corpus with `schema_version = \\\"cl.m6-corpus.v999\\\"`\\n     is rejected before any write (assert zero events written).\\n   - Classification: every record classified `BACKFILLED` with\\n     `has_authoritative_source=True`; no `UNKNOWN` for the clean corpus.\\n   - Replay: after import, `CritiqueLedgerReplay.replay_cumulative()` returns\\n     all 5 rounds' records in order.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 5: Compatibility Bridge + Fixtures\\n\\n### Step 7: faults.json dual-write bridge (`arnold_pipelines/megaplan/flags.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Medium — **Complexity: 3**\\n\\n**Rationale:** CL2 keeps `faults.json` writable for active readers during the\\ntransition window (CL5 cutover retires it). The dual-write emits a parallel WBC\\nflag-sync event so the ledger has a complete record without disrupting the\\nexisting write path.\\n\\n1. **Add** a `FlagSyncEnvelope` helper (in `arnold/critique_ledger/schemas.py`\\n   or a lightweight dict structure) that wraps the flag registry snapshot with\\n   `schema_version`, `plan_id`, `iteration`, and `flags` list.\\n2. **Add** an optional `ledger_writer` parameter to `update_flags_after_critique`\\n   (`flags.py:279-293`): when provided, after `save_flag_registry` writes\\n   `faults.json`, emit a flag-sync event via\\n   `CritiqueLedgerStore.append_flag_sync(plan_id, flags, iteration)`. This is a\\n   new method on `CritiqueLedgerStore` that serializes the flag snapshot as an\\n   `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` event with `critique_record_type = \\\"flag_sync\\\"`.\\n3. **Keep the existing path unchanged**: when `ledger_writer` is `None` (the\\n   default), behavior is identical to today. The dual-write is opt-in so existing\\n   tests and callers are unaffected.\\n4. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_flag_dual_write.py`:\\n   - With `ledger_writer=None`: `faults.json` written, no ledger event (existing\\n     behavior preserved).\\n   - With `ledger_writer`: `faults.json` written AND a flag-sync event lands in\\n     the store. Assert both paths produce consistent flag state.\\n   - `faults.json` remains writable (no read-only enforcement in CL2).\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 8: CL2 fixtures (`tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Medium — **Complexity: 3**\\n\\n1. **Create** the following fixtures (small, focused JSON files for each\\n   negative-test scenario):\\n   - `cl2_current_source.json` — a valid current-source occurrence set for\\n     positive round-trip tests.\\n   - `cl2_target_only.json` — target-schema record with no legacy source (tests\\n     forward-only import).\\n   - `cl2_future_version.json` — record declaring `\\\"cl.schema.v999\\\"` (must be\\n     rejected by the schema gate).\\n   - `cl2_corrupt.json` — structurally damaged record (missing required fields,\\n     invalid JSON structure).\\n   - `cl2_redacted.json` — record with `EvidenceAvailability.GOVERNED_REFERENCE`\\n     and redacted hashes (tests redaction handling).\\n   - `cl2_unavailable.json` — record with `EvidenceAvailability.UNAVAILABLE`\\n     and `unavailable_reason` + `reopen_condition` (tests governed tombstone).\\n   - `cl2_duplicate.json` — two records with the same `occurrence_id` (tests\\n     idempotency-key dedup → no-op).\\n2. Each fixture carries `schema_version: \\\"cl.schema.v1\\\"` unless the fixture's\\n   purpose is to test version rejection.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 6: Hardening + Handoff\\n\\n### Step 9: Negative + property tests (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_negative.py`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Medium — **Complexity: 3**\\n\\n1. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_negative.py` proving:\\n   - **No-mutation**: replay/read operations do not modify the store (row counts,\\n     `event_count`, `last_sequence` unchanged after replay). Use the\\n     `test_zero_write_mutation_gate` pattern — assert no filesystem writes, no\\n     subprocess calls during replay.\\n   - **No-partial-publication**: if a manifest references an occurrence that was\\n     never appended, `publish_manifest` raises and no manifest is visible to\\n     readers.\\n   - **Duplicate no-op**: appending the same occurrence twice returns\\n     `is_duplicate=True`; the store has exactly one event for that idempotency\\n     key.\\n   - **Post-terminal rejection**: after a `COMPLETED`/`FAILED` terminal event,\\n     appending another critique event raises `PostTerminalAppendError`.\\n   - **Monotonic sequence**: appending an event with a regressed sequence raises\\n     `MonotonicSequenceError`.\\n   - **Out-of-order**: appending reconciliation before its referenced occurrence\\n     is admitted (events are independent), but manifest publication fails if the\\n     occurrence is missing.\\n   - **Concurrent**: two writer threads/processes appending to the same\\n     `attempt_id` — one succeeds, the other either deduplicates or raises\\n     `SQLITE_BUSY` (retry handles it). No split-brain.\\n   - **Crash**: simulate a crash mid-transaction (close conn before COMMIT) — on\\n     reopen, the store is consistent (either fully committed or nothing).\\n2. **Load fixtures** from Step 8 to drive corrupt/future-version/unavailable\\n   scenarios.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Step 10: CL2 handoff document (`docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json`)\\n\\n**Scope:** Small — **Complexity: 2**\\n\\n1. **Create** `docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json` with:\\n   - `schema`: `\\\"cl.handoff.v2\\\"`.\\n   - `api_and_schema_hashes`: SHA-256 of `ledger_serializer.py`,\\n     `critique_ledger_store.py`, `critique_ledger_replay.py`,\\n     `critique_ledger_importer.py`, and the unchanged CL1 schemas.\\n   - `backend_atomicity_decision`: document SQLite WAL + `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` +\\n     shared `store.conn` as the one-transaction substrate; note the\\n     `revision_manifests` and `round_attempts` tables as cross-attempt\\n     projections (no authority).\\n   - `one_time_import_map`: M6 corpus round → occurrence/disposition counts,\\n     classification breakdown, import report hash.\\n   - `replay_hashes`: per-revision and cumulative replay projection hashes\\n     (proves byte-equivalence).\\n   - `fixture_index`: list of all CL2 fixtures with their SHA-256.\\n   - `freshness_rules`: input-set hash, prior-revision hash chain, domain\\n     completeness, WBC receipt refs.\\n   - `open_gates`: the 5 CL1 blockers carried forward (tracked, not resolved).\\n   - `backup_restore_prerequisites`: SQLite WAL checkpoint, `-wal`/`-shm` files,\\n     payload store DB.\\n   - `unresolved_limits`: dual-write is active (CL5 retires it), domain replay\\n     filters in-memory.\\n2. **Create** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_handoff.py` assertions\\n   (extend Step 1's file or add a `TestCL2HandoffDocument` class) that validate\\n   the CL2 handoff structure and hash freshness.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Execution Order\\n\\n1. **Step 1** (gate) — confirm the frozen CL1 contract before building on it.\\n2. **Step 2** (serializer) — the pure foundation everything else depends on.\\n3. **Step 3** (schema + occurrence write) — the core persistence path.\\n4. **Step 4** (recon/disposition + manifest) — completes the write path.\\n5. **Step 5** (replay) — proves the write path is correct via byte-equivalence.\\n6. **Step 6** (M6 import) — exercises the full pipeline against real data.\\n7. **Step 7** (dual-write) — compatibility bridge (lower risk, can parallelize).\\n8. **Step 8** (fixtures) — input for negative tests.\\n9. **Step 9** (negative tests) — proves invariants hold under fault.\\n10. **Step 10** (handoff) — documents the result for CL3.\\n\\nSteps 7–8 may be done in parallel with Steps 5–6 since they have no\\ndependencies on the replay/importer implementation.\\n\\n## Validation Order\\n\\n1. **Step 1**: `pytest tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_m6_importer.py tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_m6_corpus.py` — import + corpus integrity.\\n6. **Step 7**: `pytest `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/` suite — confirm no regressions in existing tests (`test_semantic_loop`, `test_semantic_loop_failures`, `test_policy_tables`, `test_schemas`).\\n\\n## Success Should CL2 treat this as a conditional bridge (proceed, track blockers as open gates) or block until formal acceptance? The plan assumes conditional bridge — is that acceptable?\\\"\\n  impact: \\\"Determines whether Steps 3-10 proceed at all or are deferred indefinitely.\\\"\\n- question: \\\"Should the faults.json dual-write (Step 7) be wired into the existing update_flags_after_critique call site during CL2, or only available as an opt-in hook for explicit CL2 callers? The plan defaults to opt-in (ledger_writer=None preserves existing behavior) to avoid disrupting non-CL2 callers.\\\"\\n  impact: \\\"Determines whether existing megaplan callers need modification or the bridge is purely additive.\\\"\\n- question: \\\"For round_attempts population inside the occurrence append transaction: the outbox's append_event_with_outbox manages its own BEGIN IMMEDIATE/COMMIT. Should CritiqueLedgerStore reimplement the append transaction to include the round_attempts INSERT, or add a transaction-callback hook to the outbox? The plan assumes reimplementation in CritiqueLedgerStore to avoid modifying the frozen outbox API.\\\"\\n  impact: \\\"Determines whether ledger_outbox.py is modified (risky) or critique_ledger_store.py owns its own transaction (safer, more code).\\\"\\n```\\n\\n## Assumptions\\n\\n```yaml\\n- assumption: \\\"CL2 proceeds conditionally against the frozen CL1 schemas and evidence. The 5 CL1 blockers are procedural/infrastructural and do not invalidate the frozen schemas, corpus, oracle, or semantic loop. CL2 tracks them as open gates. If any blocker resolution alters schemas, CL2 scope recalibrates.\\\"\\n- assumption: \\\"Critique domain records serialize through LedgerEvent.payload using existing generic event types (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT for occurrences, RECONCILIATION for reconciliations, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME for dispositions, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT for manifests). A discriminator field (critique_record_type) in the payload wrapper makes deserialization unambiguous without overloading event_type semantics or widening the enum.\\\"\\n- assumption: \\\"The revision_manifests and round_attempts tables are separate projections in the same SQLite DB, populated atomically on store.conn. They carry no positive authority — they are rebuildable indexes that enable cross-attempt aggregation without widening attempt_events.\\\"\\n- assumption: \\\"The CritiqueLedgerStore reimplements the append transaction (mirroring outbox's dedup → post-terminal → monotonic → INSERT → COMMIT sequence) to include round_attempts population, rather than modifying the frozen SqliteLedgerOutbox API.\\\"\\n- assumption: \\\"Domain-bounded replay filters in-memory after loading round/cumulative events, because domain cardinality is bounded (2-4 per briefing budget) and domain lives in the occurrence envelope, not a column.\\\"\\n- assumption: \\\"M6 corpus records classify as BACKFILLED (has_authoritative_source=True) because the corpus is frozen and hash-verified. Unmappable records classify as UNKNOWN — never BACKFILLED from circumstantial evidence.\\\"\\n- assumption: \\\"faults.json dual-write is opt-in (ledger_writer=None default). Existing callers and tests are unaffected. The bridge is active only when CL2 explicitly provides a ledger_writer.\\\"\\n```\\n\\n## Changed Surfaces\\n\\n```yaml\\nsource_files_new:\\n  - arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_serializer.py\\n  - arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_store.py\\n  - arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_replay.py\\n  - arnold/workflow/critique_ledger_importer.py\\n\\nsource_files_modified:\\n  - arnold/critique_ledger/__init__.py\\n  - arnold/critique_ledger/schemas.py\\n  - arnold_pipelines/megaplan/flags.py\\n\\ntest_files_new:\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_handoff.py\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_ledger_serializer.py\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_critique_ledger_store.py\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_critique_ledger_replay.py\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_m6_importer.py\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_flag_dual_write.py\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_negative.py\\n\\ntest_files_modified:\\n  - tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl1_handoff.py\\n\\nfixtures_new:\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_current_source.json\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_target_only.json\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_future_version.json\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_corrupt.json\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_redacted.json\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_unavailable.json\\n  - tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_duplicate.json\\n\\ndocs_new:\\n  - docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json\\n```\\n\\n## Test Blast Radius\\n\\n```json\\n{\\n  \\\"strategy\\\": \\\"scoped\\\",\\n  \\\"confidence\\\": \\\"high\\\",\\n  \\\"selectors\\\": [\\n    {\\n      \\\"kind\\\": \\\"path\\\",\\n      \\\"value\\\": \\\"tests/arnold/critique_ledger/\\\",\\n      \\\"reason\\\": \\\"All CL2 tests (new and existing) live here; The plan is written and complete. Here is a summary of what I produced.

## Plan Summary

**Title:** CL2 — WBC-backed Ledger Persistence, Import, and Replay

I explored the repository to verify every claim in the engineering brief against actual source code, then produced a 10-step plan across 6 phases. Key findings that shaped the plan:

### Verified from source
- **No enum widening needed**: `LedgerEvent.payload` (`execution_attempt_ledger.py:755`) accepts `dict | DurableRef | None`, and `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` requires a non-null payload (`:837-842`) — so CL1 records can ride through `payload` using existing generic event types.
- **Atomicity proven**: `SqliteLedgerOutbox.append_event_with_outbox` (`:336-499`) demonstrates BEGIN IMMEDIATE → dedup → post-terminal → monotonic → INSERT event → INSERT outbox → COMMIT on the shared `store.conn`. The payload store shares the same connection.
- **Attempt-scoped table confirmed**: `attempt_events` has `PRIMARY KEY (attempt_id, sequence)` with no domain/round columns — cross-attempt manifest aggregation genuinely needs separate tables.
- **Purity invariant**: `arnold.critique_ledger` is documented as pure/side-effect-free — so the serializer goes there, while the WBC-backed persistence/replay/importer go in `arnold.workflow`.

### Explicitly flagged tension
The CL1 handoff has `accepted_for_cl2: false` with 5 blockers, but the constraint says "Consume only an accepted CL1 handoff." I surfaced this as a **conditional bridge** (not a silent override): all must-gates pass, schemas are frozen with verifiable hashes, and the 5 blockers are procedural. CL2 proceeds conditionally, tracked by a dedicated gate test (Step 1) and carried forward in the handoff.

### Phased structure (each step ≤ one worker turn)
1. **Foundation**: conditional-acceptance gate + pure serializer (Complexity 2, 3)
2. **Persistence**: schema DDL + occurrence append/start-gate, then recon/disposition + manifest publication (Complexity 4, 3)
3. **Replay**: store-backed read-only replay delegating to the pure `replay_full` (Complexity 4)
4. **Import**: M6 one-time importer with LegacyClassification (Complexity 4)
5. **Compatibility + fixtures**: faults.json dual-write bridge + 7 fixtures (Complexity 3, 3)
6. **Hardening + handoff**: negative/property tests + CL2 handoff doc (Complexity 3, 2)

The plan includes 16 `must` success criteria with concrete verification requirements, 3 material questions, 7 stated assumptions, a complete `changed_surfaces` list (4 new source files, 3 modified, 7 new test files, 7 fixtures, 1 handoff doc), and a scoped `test_blast_radius` with full-suite fallback.
