### Epic North Star: North Star: Context-First Critique with Cumulative Finding Custody
Source: `.megaplan/initiatives/critique-ledger/NORTHSTAR.md`
Captured artifact: `anchors/north_star/epic.md`
Checksum: `35c76087dc3a70924f06c1446a882d8f28c4679db42d0fe266f5046b22662143`

```md
# North Star: Context-First Critique with Cumulative Finding Custody

## Destination

Every Megaplan critique loop preserves what every selected critic found, what
the evaluator concluded, what revision action followed, and why a finding is
open, resolved, rejected, deferred, duplicated, accepted as risk, or unknown.
Later rounds receive bounded domain context without erasing raw evidence or
pressuring critics to invent novelty.

The evaluator remains the semantic routing and reconciliation authority. WBC
and Megaplan custody mechanisms provide durable attempt/evidence boundaries;
the critique ledger never becomes an execution authority, lifecycle writer,
repair queue, or substitute for raw producer artifacts.

## Load-bearing invariants

1. Every critic attempt and parseable occurrence is immutable and addressable,
   including failed attempts and valid `no_additional_findings` results.
2. One cumulative logical finding set has bounded per-domain projections; a
   semantic merge never destroys or rewrites occurrences.
3. Each known finding has an explicit current disposition representing
   acted-on, ignored/non-action, deferred, rejected, duplicate, accepted-risk,
   unknown, or resolved semantics, with rationale and evidence limits.
4. Resolution and non-action include explicit reopen conditions. Relevant plan,
   evidence, contract, or repository changes make stale closure fail visible.
5. Semantic equivalence, refinement, regression, split, and reopen decisions
   are model judgments preserved as append-only reconciliation events.
6. Deterministic machinery proves custody, completeness, freshness,
   idempotency, schema/version compatibility, and exact input/output hashes.
7. The evaluator selects domain critics. Each history-aware pass receives all
   relevant findings—including non-blocking and cross-domain findings—within a
   declared budget and with explicit overflow behavior.
8. Blind discovery is optional; history-aware reconciliation is mandatory
   before revise/gate consumption.
9. `no_additional_findings`, `no_open_blocking_findings`, `no_known_findings`,
   and `no_adjacent_text_matches` remain distinct claims.
10. The reviser records one action or explicit non-action per requested finding;
    the gate sees complete cumulative truth and cannot infer absence from an
    omitted prompt row.
11. WBC owns supported-runtime attempt/effect evidence, durable payload
    references, receipts, persistence, and compatibility boundaries. The
    critique ledger owns immutable critic occurrences, semantic finding
    identities, disposition/reopen events, bounded history briefings, and
    rebuildable derived projections.
12. Existing Megaplan components retain critic selection, revision, gate, and
    lifecycle authority. The migration switches the complete critique loop at
    one coordinated cutover and then retires the replaced path; custody failure
    stops admission rather than falling back silently or declaring convergence.

## Success measures

- Zero dropped flagged producer outputs and 100% reconciliation coverage.
- The M6 blocked-handoff family reconstructs as one finding with five retained
  occurrences while the accepted replay limitation retains its disposition.
- At least 50% fewer duplicate revision actions than control.
- No more than a five-percentage-point loss in independent new-family recall.
- At least 95% of closure/non-action dispositions cite adequate evidence.
- Stale briefings, missing occurrences, invalid schemas, unsupported closures,
  and incomplete WBC custody fail closed before revise/gate behavior changes.
- Minimum cutover evidence is durable and attributable to exact source,
  implementation, corpus, schema, model, and profile revisions.
- After cutover there is one supported critique-ledger architecture, no active
  legacy consumption path, and projections rebuild from WBC-backed evidence.

## Anti-scope

- No embedding database, general semantic graph, project-memory platform, or
  hard-coded similarity threshold as semantic authority.
- No parallel execution ledger, authority plane, transition writer, repair
  queue, or lifecycle state.
- No silent truncation, historical backfill that invents semantic relations,
  automatic closure from wording similarity, or severity/disposition collapse.
- No canary, prolonged shadow authority, dual-write period, broad mixed-version
  support, per-boundary rollback, or rollout dashboard program.
- No deployment, restart, chain launch, or runtime mutation by these planning
  assets themselves.
```

You are creating an implementation plan for the following idea.

        Engineering brief produced from the codebase and task details:

        ### Task Summary
        Implement WBC-backed ledger persistence, import, and replay for the critique ledger by consuming the frozen CL1 contract through existing SqliteAttemptLedgerStore, SqliteLedgerOutbox, and SqliteLedgerPayloadStore seams. Append CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, FindingReconciliationEvent, and FindingDispositionEvent records atomically with DurableRef payload references; publish LedgerRevisionManifest only after complete occurrence accounting validates; build cumulative domain projections and deterministic read-only replay; and execute a one-time import from the retained M6 corpus (5 rounds, zero failures) into the CL1 schema target. The WBC substrate already satisfies the one-transaction atomicity requirement (SQLite WAL + BEGIN IMMEDIATE + shared connection), but two gaps must be bridged: critique domain records must route through existing generic AttemptEventType values via LedgerEvent.payload without extending the enum, and cross-attempt LedgerRevisionManifest aggregation must be implemented as a separate mapping layer since the store is attempt-scoped. The CL1 handoff is formally not accepted (accepted_for_cl2: false, 5 procedural blockers) but all must-gates pass and all material artifacts—schemas, corpus, oracle, semantic loop—are frozen with verifiable hashes. CL2 can proceed conditionally, tracking the 5 blockers as open gates.

        ### Key Evidence
        - SQLite WAL + BEGIN IMMEDIATE + shared connection across SqliteAttemptLedgerStore, SqliteLedgerOutbox, and SqliteLedgerPayloadStore satisfies the one-transaction atomicity requirement. The outbox's append_event_with_outbox already demonstrates atomic event+outbox writes in one BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction; the payload store shares the same store.conn and can participate in the same atomic boundary. (source: arnold/workflow/ledger_outbox.py:336-499, arnold/workflow/ledger_payload_store.py:683-693, arnold/workflow/attempt_ledger_store.py:1028-1143; relevance: high)
- All five CL1 schemas are frozen at cl.schema.v1 with SHA-256 hashes recorded in the handoff. CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope carries occurrence_id, finding_id, semantic_finding_id as three distinct identity fields; FindingReconciliationEvent maps multiple occurrence_ids to one canonical_finding_id; FindingDispositionEvent assigns one of 8 disposition families with severity and reopen_predicate; LedgerRevisionManifest requires input_set_hash, prior_revision_hash, domain_completeness, and wbc_receipt_refs. (source: docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl1-contract-oracle.json:9-57, arnold/critique_ledger/schemas.py:182-731; relevance: high)
- CL1 handoff accepted_for_cl2 is false with 5 blocking reasons, but all CL1 must-gates pass (custody, freshness, replay equality, authority, schema, prerequisite). The semantic loop gate verdict is accepted:true with zero replay failures and zero-write assertion verified. The 5 blockers are procedural/infrastructural—missing reviewer sign-off, M6 prerequisite INCOHERENT (M5 attestation gap), proof-index downstream failure, 4 Run Authority ownership blockers, portfolio gate PC-SCOPE-BLOCKER-001—not material invalidations of the frozen schemas or evidence. (source: docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl1-contract-oracle.json:411-441, docs/critique-ledger/evidence/cl1-semantic-loop-gate.json:51-73; relevance: high)
- faults.json is the sole plan-local file read by multiple phases (critique, gate, finalize, review, override) via load_flag_registry/save_flag_registry. It must remain writable during CL2 and cannot become a read-only projection until coordinated cutover (CL5). Critique custody artifacts (critique_custody_v{N}.json, critique_clearance.json) are dual-writable during CL2 and become read-only projections after cutover. All other ~55 plan-local files (state.json, contract.json, gate_signals_v{N}.json, etc.) are out of scope for CL2. (source: arnold_pipelines/megaplan/_core/registries.py:31-39, arnold_pipelines/megaplan/flags.py:279-293, arnold_pipelines/megaplan/orchestration/critique_custody.py:208-291,530-634; relevance: high)
- M6 corpus is complete (892KB, 5 rounds v1-v5, 12 artifact types per round) with overall=complete, zero missing_required_artifacts, zero unavailable_evidence. Oracle fact 6 confirms zero FAILED/DROPPED/MALFORMED instances across all rounds. The partial-parse contract is defined by the failure-atomicity-table: ParseStatus.FAILED records attempt but emits no findings; MALFORMED preserves evidence as UNAVAILABLE with reopen condition; TOMBSTONED marks record removed. LegacyClassification enum (BACKFILLED/UNKNOWN/CORRUPT/UNRECONSTRUCTABLE) enforces forward-fix-only—only has_authoritative_source permits BACKFILLED. (source: docs/critique-ledger/evidence/m6-corpus-manifest.json, docs/critique-ledger/evidence/m6-oracle.json:72-86, docs/critique-ledger/evidence/failure-atomicity-table.json, arnold/workflow/ledger_migrations.py:190-270; relevance: high)
- Per-attempt event stream replay (pattern a) is fully covered by PRIMARY KEY (attempt_id, sequence) via read_events(). Domain-bounded replay (b) and round-scoped replay (c) have NO existing index support—domain and round_label are critique-ledger concepts stored in CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, not in the WBC attempt_events table. Cumulative projection rebuild (d) requires a standalone sequence index since PRIMARY KEY groups by attempt_id first, preventing efficient cross-attempt ordered scans. Both a round_attempts mapping table (avoiding schema widening) and a standalone sequence index are achievable within the two-week scope. (source: arnold/workflow/attempt_ledger_store.py:73-88,1199-1226, arnold/critique_ledger/schemas.py:182-312, arnold/critique_ledger/semantic_loop.py:964-1206; relevance: high)
- No critique-specific AttemptEventType values exist—the enum has 11 generic lifecycle types (STARTED, COMPLETED, FAILED, RETRY_SCHEDULED, SUSPENDED, RESUMED, CANCELLED, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME, PERSISTENCE_FAILED, RECONCILIATION). The CL1 constraint forbids widening the schema without a reviewed decision. However, LedgerEvent.payload accepts arbitrary dict or DurableRef, so critique domain records can serialize through payload using existing event types (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT for occurrences, RECONCILIATION for reconciliation, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME for dispositions). (source: arnold/workflow/execution_attempt_ledger.py:146-164, arnold/workflow/attempt_ledger_store.py:1028-1143; relevance: high)

CL2 must track this as a conditional-acceptance gate—all must-gates pass, schemas are frozen, but formal acceptance is pending.
- [pass_to_pass] test_m6_corpus: Validates M6 corpus completeness, dual hashing, round coverage, artifact availability, and redaction correctness. Must pass—corpus is the import source.
- [pass_to_pass] test_semantic_loop: Validates deterministic replay: five occurrences reconcile to one semantic finding, replay twice produces identical hashes, zero-write assertion holds. Must pass—proves the replay contract CL2 must preserve when moving from in-memory to store-backed replay.
- [pass_to_pass] test_semantic_loop_failures: Validates closed-failure behavior: missing custody, invalid schema versions, corrupt evidence all fail cleanly without partial writes. CL2 must extend these to cover store-backed failure modes (PostTerminalAppendError, MonotonicSequenceError, persistence_failed).
- [pass_to_pass] test_policy_tables: Validates atomicity/failure table and privacy/retention table against landed WBC policy. Must pass—these tables define the persistence contract CL2 implements.
- [pass_to_pass] test_zero_write_mutation_gate: Validates semantic loop functions are pure—zero filesystem writes, zero subprocess calls. Must pass—CL2 replay must match this purity constraint (no model calls, no external effects).

        ### Constraints
        - Consume only the frozen CL1 handoff schemas and hashes—do not widen schema or ownership without a reviewed CL1 decision. This means routing critique domain records through existing AttemptEventType values (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, RECONCILIATION, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME) via LedgerEvent.payload, not extending the enum.
- All writes are idempotent, content-safe, access-controlled, and fault-tested. The 4 Step 4 invariants (monotonic sequence, idempotency-key dedup, exactly-one-terminal, post-terminal rejection) are already enforced by _append_tx—CL2 must not bypass them.
- Start persistence precedes critic dispatch; terminal persistence failure is visible persistence_failed|indeterminate, never clean success. The failure-atomicity-table governs all 13 failure modes.
- Occurrences are never merged away. Semantic identity and occurrence identity remain distinct. Projections are rebuildable and carry no positive authority.
- Historical gaps stay explicit; migration never invents duplicate, resolution, evidence, or reopen relationships. LegacyClassification enforces forward-fix-only.
- Replay issues no model call or external effect. The semantic loop's zero-write assertion (verified by test_zero_write_mutation_gate) must hold for store-backed replay.
- faults.json must remain writable by critique and gate phases during CL2. The coordinated cutover to read-only projection is CL5 scope. Custody artifacts are dual-written during CL2.
- No new database service, event bus, authority plane, generalized knowledge store, embeddings, broad historical backfill, dual-write window, or old-reader retirement before CL5.
- Every accepted producer occurrence appears once; duplicate replay is a no-op; partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete ledger revision. The idempotency-key dedup (checked before rejection) and post-terminal rejection enforce this.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Phase 1—Bridge critique domain to WBC event stream: Build a CritiqueLedgerSerializer that converts CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope → LedgerEvent (event_type=EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, payload=serialized envelope), FindingReconciliationEvent → LedgerEvent (event_type=RECONCILIATION, payload=serialized event), FindingDispositionEvent → LedgerEvent (event_type=EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME, payload=serialized event). Construct DurableRef instances for evidence payloads per the retention-class-mapping (occurrence envelopes: confidential/run; reconciliation/disposition: restricted/audit; manifests: restricted/audit). Wire through SqliteLedgerOutbox.append_event_with_outbox for atomic event+payload persistence. No enum extension needed—payload carries the typed critique record.

Phase 2—Implement occurrence append with start-persistence gate: Before critic dispatch, call _append_tx with a STARTED event. On terminal persistence failure, emit PERSISTENCE_FAILED (not clean success). Append each accepted occurrence as an EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT event with the serialized CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope in payload. Append reconciliation and disposition events after complete occurrence accounting validates. Only after all events for a revision are appended, construct and append a LedgerRevisionManifest event.

Phase 3—Cross-attempt LedgerRevisionManifest: Implement a revision_manifests table (revision_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, prior_revision_hash TEXT, input_set_hash TEXT, domain_completeness TEXT, wbc_receipt_refs TEXT, event_ids TEXT, created_at_ns INTEGER) in the same SQLite database. After all occurrences/reconciliations/dispositions for a revision are atomically appended, INSERT the manifest in the same transaction. This avoids widening attempt_events.

Phase 4—Replay indexes: Create a round_attempts mapping table (round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)) populated at occurrence-write time from CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label. Add CREATE INDEX idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence) for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans. Per-attempt replay uses existing PRIMARY KEY; domain/round replay joins round_attempts → attempt_events; cumulative rebuild scans idx_attempt_events_sequence.

Phase 5—One-time M6 import: Traverse m6-corpus.json rounds v1-v5. For each round: map critique_custody.findings[] to CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope instances, evaluator_verdict.flag_verifications[] to FindingReconciliationEvent/DispositionEvent instances, plan-level faults/contract to LedgerRevisionManifest inputs. Classify every legacy record through LegacyClassification: has_authoritative_source → BACKFILLED; otherwise UNKNOWN or UNRECONSTRUCTABLE. Apply the partial-parse contract from failure-atomicity-table even though the corpus has zero failures. Reject unsupported schema versions via SUPPORTED_VERSIONS gate before any write. Import into the WBC store using the same atomic append path—each imported occurrence is an immutable event. Mark all imported records with metadata.source='m6-corpus-one-time-import'.

Phase 6—Faults.json dual-write: Keep save_flag_registry writing to faults.json unchanged. Add a parallel WBC write: after update_flags_after_critique upserts FlagRecords into the registry, also emit a critique-ledger flag-sync event (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT with a FlagSyncEnvelope payload) into the WBC store. This dual-writes faults.json (for active readers during CL2) and the ledger (for eventual cutover to read-only projection in CL5).

Phase 7—Fixtures and negative tests: Add all required fixtures (current-source import, target-only, future-version, corrupt, redacted, unavailable, duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, crash). Prove negative tests: ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue, Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state. Prove duplicate replay is a no-op via idempotency-key dedup. Prove partial publication cannot expose incomplete revision (post-terminal rejection + atomic outbox).

### Open Questions
- [assume_and_proceed] The CL1 handoff has accepted_for_cl2: false with 5 procedural blockers (missing reviewer sign-off, M6 prerequisite INCOHERENT, proof-index FAILED, 4 ownership blockers, portfolio gate PC-SCOPE-BLOCKER-001). The CL2 constraint states 'Consume only an accepted CL1 handoff.' Can CL2 proceed against the frozen schemas and evidence while the handoff remains formally unaccepted? _(assumption: All CL1 must-gates pass (custody, freshness, replay equality, authority, schema, prerequisite). The five schemas are frozen with SHA-256 hashes in the handoff and independently verifiable from disk. The M6 corpus, manifest, and oracle are complete and hash-verified. The 5 blocking reasons are procedural/infrastructural—they do not invalidate the CL1 work product. CL2 proceeds conditionally, tracking the 5 blockers as open gates tracked in the CL2 handoff, with the understanding that schema hashes must not change and any blocker resolution that alters schemas would require CL2 scope recalibration.)_
- [assume_and_proceed] The WBC AttemptEventType enum has no critique-specific event types (only 11 generic lifecycle types), but the CL1 constraint forbids widening the schema without a reviewed CL1 decision. How are CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, FindingReconciliationEvent, and FindingDispositionEvent represented in the WBC event stream? _(assumption: Critique domain records serialize through LedgerEvent.payload (which accepts arbitrary dict or DurableRef) using existing generic event types: EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT for CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope, RECONCILIATION for FindingReconciliationEvent, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME for FindingDispositionEvent, and a LedgerRevisionManifest-specific event routed through EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT as well. The payload field carries the full typed record. This avoids enum extension entirely—the event_type signals the lifecycle role, the payload carries the domain-specific content. Deserialization reconstructs the typed critique record from payload by inspecting the schema_version field.)_
- [assume_and_proceed] The WBC attempt_events table is attempt-scoped with PRIMARY KEY (attempt_id, sequence). The LedgerRevisionManifest spans multiple attempts and requires cross-attempt aggregation. No cross-attempt table or aggregation mechanism currently exists. Can CL2 implement this within the two-week scope without widening attempt_events? _(assumption: Implement a separate revision_manifests table in the same SQLite database (revision_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, prior_revision_hash, input_set_hash, domain_completeness, wbc_receipt_refs, event_ids, created_at_ns). The table is populated atomically in the same transaction as the final event of a revision using the shared store.conn. This avoids widening attempt_events and keeps the domain-specific aggregation separate from the generic event stream—consistent with the locked decision that projections carry no positive authority.)_
- [assume_and_proceed] Domain-bounded and round-scoped replay have no existing index support in the WBC store—domain and round_label exist only in CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope (event_json), not as columns in attempt_events. The constraint forbids widening the attempt_events schema. What is the minimum viable approach within the two-week scope? _(assumption: Create a round_attempts mapping table (round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)) populated at occurrence-write time from CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label. Domain-bounded replay extends this pattern with a domain_attempts table if needed, or filters in memory after loading round-scoped events since domain cardinality is bounded (2-4 domains per briefing budget). Add CREATE INDEX idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence) for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans. This avoids widening attempt_events while enabling all four required replay patterns within the two-week scope.)_

        Idea:
# CL2 — WBC-backed ledger persistence, import, and replay

## Outcome

Implement the CL1 record contract through existing WBC/plan-local persistence
seams, with atomic append, idempotent retry, rebuildable projections, read-only
replay, and one-time import from retained legacy artifacts. Leave CL3 one stable
target API and fixtures.

## In scope

- Persist critic start/result and immutable occurrence envelopes with WBC
  attempt/payload references and current raw/custody artifacts.
- Append reconciliation/disposition events and publish a ledger revision only
  after complete occurrence accounting validates.
- Build cumulative/domain projections and deterministic read-only replay.
- Add current-source import, target-only, future-version, corrupt, redacted,
  unavailable, duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, and crash fixtures.
- Add the target schema reader and explicit `legacy_unknown` import behavior;
  retain current flag/gate/finalize inputs only until the coordinated cutover.
- Implement freshness vectors and governed tombstone/unavailable handling.

## Out of scope

Evaluator selection, critic prompt changes, semantic auto-merge policy, reviser
or gate consumption, live cutover, old artifact deletion, or production
backfill of historical semantic relationships.

## Locked decisions

- Start persistence precedes critic dispatch; terminal persistence failure is
  visible `persistence_failed|indeterminate`, never clean success.
- Occurrences are never merged away. Semantic identity and occurrence identity
  remain distinct.
- Projections are rebuildable and carry no positive authority.
- Historical gaps stay explicit; migration never invents duplicate, resolution,
  evidence, or reopen relationships.
- WBC inline/reference, retention, redaction, ordering, and effect rules are
  reused, not reimplemented.

## Open questions

- Which landed backend satisfies one transaction, and where is durable
  prepare/outbox reconciliation required?
- What plan-local compatibility files remain necessary for old readers?
- How are partially parseable failed attempts admitted without promoting an
  invalid whole payload?
- What indexes are necessary within the two-week scope for domain/round replay?

## Constraints

Consume only an accepted CL1 handoff. Do not widen schema or ownership without a
new reviewed CL1 decision. All writes are idempotent, content-safe, access-
controlled, and fault-tested. Replay issues no model call or external effect.

## Done criteria

- Schema/golden, property, concurrency, crash/fault, compatibility, privacy,
  WBC ledger/evidence, and existing critique-custody suites pass.
- Every accepted producer occurrence appears once; duplicate replay is a no-op;
  partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete ledger revision.
- Projections rebuild byte-equivalently from retained inputs and surface
  missing/redacted evidence as unknown.
- The one-time importer preserves all available legacy evidence, marks semantic
  gaps unknown, and rejects unsupported target versions without partial publish.
- Negative tests prove ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue,
  Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state.

## Touchpoints

`arnold/workflow/execution_attempt_ledger.py`, `payload_policy.py`, boundary
evidence/compatibility/conformance; Megaplan critique custody/runtime, schemas,
artifact writers, flag registry readers, and focused WBC/critique tests.

## Anti-scope

No new database service, event bus, authority plane, generalized knowledge
store, embeddings, broad historical backfill, dual-write window, or old-reader
retirement before CL5.

## Written handoff to CL3

Write and review `docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json` with API/
schema hashes, backend/atomicity decision, one-time import map, replay hashes,
fixture index, freshness rules, fault results, backup/restore prerequisites, and
unresolved limits. CL3 must validate the handoff and replay fixtures before
constructing a briefing.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - Size each step so it can be implemented AND verified in a single worker turn (~15 min, one model conversation). A step that bundles "create the abstraction AND migrate every consumer AND write all the tests" is too large to finish in one turn and will fail (the worker runs out of time before it can implement, leaving the step blocked). For large mechanical refactors, split along the natural seam — one step to add the new abstraction + its unit tests, then one step per consumer to migrate (the last migration step also adds parity tests) — rather than one giant step. Higher complexity does not earn a step more time; it only routes to a stronger model for the same single turn.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.
        - Assign every plan step a complexity score 1–5 before finalize. Reason about complexity in the plan markdown (e.g. "Complexity: 2" per step), and surface it informally so the reviewer can audit:
          - 1 = trivial single-file mechanical change
          - 2 = simple change with tests to update
          - 3 = multi-file change with non-trivial logic
          - 4 = cross-cutting change with architecture implications
          - 5 = fundamental system change with high regression risk
        - Populate `changed_surfaces` with every concrete file path your plan will change or create. Include both source files and test files. Use repo-relative paths (e.g., `src/prompts.py`, `tests/test_prompts.py`). This list drives the deterministic test-selection blast radius; be complete. If your plan touches a file, list it.
        - (Optional) Populate `test_blast_radius` with your own scoped test-selection proposal. This complements the deterministic floor the system computes from `changed_surfaces`; you cannot narrow below the floor, but you can widen with additional selectors or escalate to full. If you intend a scoped finalize baseline while keeping the full suite as a hard gate, set `strategy` to "scoped", include concrete `selectors`, and set `full_suite_fallback` to true; the floor's full-suite requirement will be honored by the fallback without forcing the baseline strategy to "full".
        - When you include `test_blast_radius`, it MUST be schema-complete. Include every
field in this shape:
`{"strategy":"scoped","confidence":"high","selectors":[{"kind":"path","value":"tests/test_relevant.py","reason":"covers the changed surface"}],"changed_surfaces":["src/relevant.py"],"always_run":[],"full_suite_fallback":true,"rationale":"Why these tests cover the planned changes.","import_graph":{"degraded":false,"dependent_tests":0,"unresolved":[]}}`.
The runtime deterministically recomputes and merges the import graph and the
coverage floor from `changed_surfaces`; do not omit `confidence`, `always_run`,
`changed_surfaces`, or `import_graph` merely because those values are also
runtime-derived.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`src/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`src/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`src/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`src/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`src/handlers.py`, `src/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`src/handlers.py:400`, `src/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_validator.py`, `tests/test_integration.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_validator.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_integration.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

intent and outcome evidence for\n    36|  external side effects.\n    37|\n    38|Persistence-failure and reconciliation diagnostics are captured via:\n    39|\n    40|* ``PersistenceFailureDiagnostic`` — failure mode, recovery evidence spool\n    41|  reference, quarantined authority advance indicator, and observed error.\n    42|* ``ReconciliationDiagnostic`` — reconciliation target (the failed event),\n    43|  outcome, recovered evidence refs, and explicit authority disposition.\n    44|\n    45|This is schema-only — no I/O, mutation, or runtime effects.\n    46|\n    47|.. _ledger-primitive-gaps:\n    48|\n    49|Current Primitive Gaps\n    50|----------------------\n    51|\n    52|The following gaps exist in the current ledger primitives and must be\n    53|addressed by future C2-C6 migration work. They are documented here so\n    54|consumers and reviewers do not mistake their absence for design intent.\n    55|\n    56|NDJSON Sequence Gaps\n    57|~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n    58|\n    59|The current ``LedgerPosition`` uses integer ``sequence`` and\n    60|``append_position`` fields. These are not backed by a real persistent\n    61|sequence generator. When the ledger is materialized to NDJSON storage\n    62|(one JSON object per line, appended), sequence gaps can arise from:\n    63|\n    64|1. **Append-without-flush**: A runtime writes an event to NDJSON but\n    65|   crashes before the fsync/close. The file may contain a truncated\n    66|   line or a partial write at EOF. The next restart sees the gap but\n    67|   has no durable record of whether the event was fully committed.\n    68|\n    69|2. **Concurrent append races**: If two runtime instances share the same\n    70|   NDJSON file without a file-level lock or append-index coordination,\n    71|   interleaved writes can produce unparseable lines and apparent\n    72|   sequence gaps.\n    73|\n    74|3. **Rotated/missing segment files**: If the NDJSON log is rotated\n    75|   (e.g., by logrotate or a retention job) without updating the\n    76|   ledger's segment manifest, events in the rotated segment appear\n    77|   missing, producing a sequence gap.\n    78|\n    79|**Mitigation path (C4-C5)**: Replace bare NDJSON appends with a\n    80|write-ahead log (WAL) or append-blob store that provides atomic\n    81|append-with-checksum, segment manifests, and gap-detection on open.\n    82|Until then, consumers MUST treat sequence gaps as ``indeterminate``\n    83|and MUST NOT silently skip them.\n    84|\n    85|Missing Transaction / Outbox Migration\n    86|~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\n    87|\n    88|The North Star mandates that internal state/result publication uses\n    89|one transaction where the store permits, or a durable outbox /\n    90|prepare-commit protocol with deterministic reconciliation. The current\n    91|ledger schema has no representation for:\n    92|\n    93|1. **Transaction boundaries**: There is no ``transaction_begin`` /\n    94|   ``transaction_commit`` / ``transaction_abort`` event type. The\n    95|   ledger cannot express that a group of events were written atomically.\n    96|\n    97|2. **Outbox records**: There is no ``OutboxRecord`` type linking a\n    98|   ledger event to an outbox message that must be delivered to a\n    99|   downstream consumer. The outbox pattern (write event + outbox message\n   100|   in one transaction, then deliver from outbox) is not modeled.\n   101|\n   102|3. **Prepare-commit protocol**: There is no ``prepare`` / ``commit`` /\n   103|   ``rollback`` event vocabulary for two-phase commit across the ledger\n   104|   and an external store.\n   105|\n   106|**Mitigation path (C4-C6)**: Add ``transaction_begin``,\n   107|``transaction_commit``, and ``transaction_abort`` event types. Add an\n   108|``OutboxRecord`` dataclass with message identity, delivery target,\n   109|idempotency key, and delivery status. Add ``prepare`` and ``commit``\n   110|event types for two-phase scenarios. Until then, consumers MUST treat\n   111|every ledger event as independently durable and MUST NOT assume\n   112|atomicity across events.\n   113|\"\"\"\n   114|\n   115|from __future__ import On terminal\\n   persistence failure it appends `PERSISTENCE_FAILED`\\n   (`persistence_status=persistence_failed|indeterminate`), **never** clean success.\\n3. **Implement** occurrence append (`append_occurrence`): writes one\\n   `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` event per accepted occurrence via\\n   `append_event_with_outbox` (atomic event + payload DurableRef + `round_attempts`\\n   row). `append_reconciliation` / `append_disposition` write\\n   `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME` events. All reuse `_append_tx` invariants.\\n4. **Implement** manifest publication `publish_revision(...)`: **only after\\n   complete occurrence accounting validates** (call a validation sub-step that\\n   mirrors `replay_full` custody + reconciliation-coverage checks; raise on\\n   `OCCURRENCE_UNMAPPED`/unresolved). In **one transaction** on `store.conn`:\\n   append the manifest `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME` event **and** insert the\\n   `revision_manifests` row, so a partial publication can never expose an\\n   accepted incomplete revision. `input_set_hash`/`prior_revision_hash` derived\\n   from the appended events.\\n5. **Test** (`test_wbc_bridge.py`): start-gate-before-occurrence; duplicate\\n   occurrence = idempotent no-op; post-terminal append rejected; manifest not\\n   published when reconciliation coverage incomplete; manifest+table insert are\\n   atomic (simulate failure between them leaves neither); partial publication\\n   cannot be read as an accepted revision.\\n\\n### Step 3: Replay indexes and the four read paths (`arnold/critique_ledger/wbc_bridge.py`)\\n**Complexity: 3**\\n\\n1. **Implement** read paths on `CritiqueLedgerStore`, all read-only and\\n   side-effect-free:\\n   - `read_attempt(attempt_id)` — per-attempt ordered events via existing PK\\n     scan (pattern a).\\n   - `read_round(round_label)` — join `round_attempts` → `attempt_events`\\n     (pattern b).\\n   - `read_domain(domain, domain_assignments)` — load relevant round/attempt\\n     events, deserialize, filter in memory by `semantic_finding_id → domain`\\n     using bounded `domain_assignments` (pattern c; no separate domain table\\n     needed since domain cardinality is bounded by briefing budgets).\\n   - `read_cumulative()` — ordered cross-attempt scan over\\n     `idx_attempt_events_sequence` (pattern d).\\n2. Each path returns deserialized CL1 records (occurrences/reconciliations/\\n   dispositions) via `ledger_event_to_record`, preserving occurrence identity\\n   distinct from semantic identity (occurrences are never merged away).\\n3. **Test** (`test_wbc_bridge.py`): all four paths return identical content for\\n   a single-round fixture; round/domain/cumulative agree with per-attempt\\n   concatenation; ordering is monotonic by sequence.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 3: Deterministic store-backed replay\\n\\n### Step 4: Store-backed replay reusing `replay_full`, plus store failure modes (`arnold/critique_ledger/store_replay.py`)\\n**Complexity: 3**\\n\\n1. **Add** `arnold/critique_ledger/store_replay.py` with\\n   `replay_from_store(store, *, round_label=None, domain=None,\\n   domain_assignments=None, ...) -> dict`. It deserializes the relevant records\\n   from the store via the Step-3 readers and calls **`semantic_loop.replay_full`\\n   unchanged** (no edits to the frozen module). Returns the same dict shape\\n   (`custody/reconciliation/disposition/manifest/briefing/projections`).\\n2. **Verify** byte-equivalence: replaying the same store twice yields identical\\n   projection hashes (reuse `canonical_hash` from `schemas.py`); store-backed\\n   replay matches an in-memory `replay_full` fed the same records.\\n3. **Extend** `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_semantic_loop_failures.py`\\n   **additively** (new test class, do not modify existing pass-to-pass cases):\\n   store-backed failure modes — `PostTerminalAppendError`,\\n   `MonotonicSequenceError`, `persistence_failed` terminal, and partial-parse\\n   contract (`FAILED` records attempt but emits no findings; `MALFORMED` →\\n   `UNAVAILABLE` with reopen condition; `TOMBSTONED` marks removed).\\n4. **Assert** zero-write purity: `replay_from_store` performs no filesystem\\n   writes and no subprocess calls (mirrors `test_zero_write_mutation_gate`\\n   semantics). No model calls, no external effects.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 4: One-time M6 import\\n\\n### Step 5: One-time importer for the retained M6 corpus (`arnold/critique_ledger/m6_importer.py`)\\n**Complexity: 4**\\n\\n1. **Add** `import_m6_corpus(store, *, corpus_path, target_schema_version)`\\n   that traverses `m6-corpus.json` rounds v1–v5. Before any write it validates\\n   `target_schema_version in SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` and **rejects** unsupported\\n   versions with **no partial publish** (write nothing).\\n2. **Map** each round: `critique_custody.findings[]` →\\n   `CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope`; `evaluator_verdict.flag_verifications[]` →\\n   `FindingReconciliationEvent`/`FindingDispositionEvent`; plan-level\\n   faults/contract → `LedgerRevisionManifest` inputs. Apply the\\n   `failure-atomicity-table.json` partial-parse contract even though the corpus\\n   has zero failures.\\n3. **Classify** every legacy record through `ledger_migrations.LegacyClassification`\\n   + `ClassificationEvidence`: `has_authoritative_source=True` (the corpus is the\\n   authoritative retained source) → `BACKFILLED`; otherwise `UNKNOWN`/`UNRECONSTRUCTABLE`.\\n   Never synthesize `BACKFILLED` from circumstantial evidence\\n   (`ClassificationSafetyError`). Mark every imported record\\n   `metadata.source = \\\"m6-corpus-one-time-import\\\"` and surface semantic gaps as\\n   `unknown` (no invented duplicate/resolution/evidence/reopen relations).\\n4. **Import atomically and idempotently** through the same `CritiqueLedgerStore`\\n   append path (Step 2). Re-running import is a no-op via idempotency-key dedup;\\n   a crash mid-import leaves a valid, completable store (each occurrence is an\\n   immutable event).\\n5. **Test** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_m6_importer.py`): all 5 rounds\\n   import; occurrence count matches corpus; re-import is a no-op; unsupported\\n   target version rejected with zero writes; `BACKFILLED` requires authoritative\\n   source; a deliberately corrupt/circumstantial-only record falls back to\\n   `UNKNOWN`/`UNRECONSTRUCTABLE`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 5: Compatibility bridge and negative-test matrix\\n\\n### Step 6: Non-authoritative faults.json / custody mirror (`arnold/critique_ledger/flag_mirror.py`, hook in `arnold_pipelines/megaplan/orchestration/critique_custody.py`)\\n**Complexity: 3** — *gated by Q2; deferrable to CL5*\\n\\n1. **Add** `mirror_flags_to_ledger(store, flag_registry, *, round_label)`\\n   in a new `flag_mirror.py` that emits a non-authoritative\\n   `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` event carrying a `FlagSyncEnvelope` payload\\n   (`cl_record_kind=\\\"flag_sync\\\"`). It **never** writes `faults.json` and carries\\n   **no positive authority** — it is rebuildable evidence only.\\n2. **Wire** a single additive, guarded call after `write_critique_clearance` /\\n   `update_flags_after_critique` in `critique_custody.py` so `faults.json`\\n   remains the authority and its existing write path is unchanged. The call must\\n   fail soft: a mirror failure must not break the active critique/gate path.\\n3. **Test** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_flag_mirror.py` +\\n   `tests/orchestration/test_critique_custody.py`): `faults.json` content\\n   unchanged by the mirror; mirror event present and rebuildable; mirror failure\\n   does not corrupt the critique path.\\n\\n### Step 7: Fixture matrix (`tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2_*.json`)\\n**Complexity: 2**\\n\\n1. **Create** the required fixtures: `cl2_current_source.json`,\\n   `cl2_target_only.json`, `cl2_future_version.json`, `cl2_corrupt.json`,\\n   `cl2_redacted.json`, `cl2_unavailable.json`, `cl2_duplicate.json`,\\n   `cl2_out_of_order.json`, `cl2_concurrent.json`, `cl2_crash.json`.\\n2. Each fixture is a minimal, hand-checkable input exercising one\\n   import/replay/failure boundary. Future-version and corrupt fixtures must be\\n   rejected by the version/discriminator gates; duplicate and out-of-order must\\n   be handled by idempotency/monotonicity; redacted/unavailable must surface as\\n   `unknown` without synthesis.\\n\\n### Step 8: Negative tests — replay/persistence cannot mutate external state (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_negative.py`)\\n**Complexity: 3**\\n\\n1. **Prove** ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue,\\n   Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state: snapshot relevant artifacts\\n   before/after each store operation and assert byte-equality; readers never return\\n   a manifest for an incomplete revision).\\n4. **Prove** the version gate and discriminator fail closed (future-version and\\n   corrupt fixtures rejected with zero writes).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 6: Handoff\\n\\n### Step 9: Write and validate the CL2 handoff (`docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json`)\\n**Complexity: 2**\\n\\n1. **Write** `cl2-ledger-replay.json` (schema `cl.handoff.v1`) with: API/schema\\n   hashes for the new modules; ### [msg 133] role=assistant ts=2026-07-24 04:30:25 UTC content=2426b finish=stop
I've written the complete implementation plan. Here's a summary of what I produced and the key decisions.

## Plan written
`/.megaplan/plans/cl2-wbc-backed-ledger-20260724-0351/plan.md`

## What I verified from the codebase (every brief claim I checked held)
- The 5 frozen CL1 dataclasses, `SUPPORTED_VERSIONS={'cl.schema.v1'}`, and the pure `replay_full`.
- `_append_tx` enforces the 4 Step-4 invariants in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; `append_event_with_outbox` proves atomic event+outbox on the shared `store.conn`.
- `LedgerEvent.payload` accepts arbitrary dict/`DurableRef`; `AttemptEventType` has 11 generic values, none critique-specific.
- `test_cl1_handoff.py` recomputes and compares SHA-256 of `schemas.py`, `semantic_loop.py`, `__init__.py`, `m6-corpus.json`, and 5 evidence JSONs → **these must not be edited**.
- The retention-class mapping, failure-atomicity-table, and `LegacyClassification` (forward-fix-only) all match the brief.

## Key evidence-based deviation from the brief
The brief's open-question #2 routes `FindingReconciliationEvent` → `RECONCILIATION`. The code disagrees: `_LIFECYCLE_PRECEDENCE` (enforced by `validate_event_ordering`) requires a preceding `PERSISTENCE_FAILED` before any `RECONCILIATION` event, which a normal critique stream lacks. I used a **precedence-safe mapping** (occurrence=`EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT`; reconciliation/disposition/manifest=`EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME`) plus a `cl_record_kind` **payload discriminator**. This is surfaced as **Q1** for confirmation.

## Anchor conflict surfaced (not silently resolved)
The brief authorizes a faults.json/custody "dual-write" while the Epic anti-scope forbids a "dual-write period." I scoped Step 6 as a **non-authoritative mirror** (ledger carries no positive authority; `faults.json` stays sole authority) and marked it **deferrable to CL5** if a reviewer reads the anti-scope strictly (**Q2**).

## Structure
9 steps across 6 phases, each sized to one ~15-min worker turn (complexities 2–4). CL2 proceeds **conditionally** against frozen CL1 schemas/evidence with the 5 CL1 blockers tracked as open gates. New code is additive (no edits to frozen sources, no enum/`attempt_events` widening); store replay reuses `replay_full` unchanged.

Three material questions, full `changed_surfaces`, schema-complete `test_blast_radius` (scoped, full-suite fallback), and 14 success criteria (11 `must`, 2 `should`, 1 `info`) are all included.

### Epic North Star: North Star: Context-First Critique with Cumulative Finding Custody
Source: `.megaplan/initiatives/critique-ledger/NORTHSTAR.md`

```md
# North Star: Context-First Critique with Cumulative Finding Custody

## Destination

Every Megaplan critique loop preserves what every selected critic found, what
the evaluator concluded, what revision action followed, and why a finding is
open, resolved, rejected, deferred, duplicated, accepted as risk, or unknown.
Later rounds receive bounded domain context without erasing raw evidence or
pressuring critics to invent novelty.

The evaluator remains the semantic routing and reconciliation authority. WBC
and Megaplan custody mechanisms provide durable attempt/evidence boundaries;
the critique ledger never becomes an execution authority, lifecycle writer,
repair queue, or substitute for raw producer artifacts.

## Load-bearing invariants

1. Every critic attempt and parseable occurrence is immutable and addressable,
   including failed attempts and valid `no_additional_findings` results.
2. One cumulative logical finding set has bounded per-domain projections; a
   semantic merge never destroys or rewrites occurrences.
3. Each known finding has an explicit current disposition representing
   acted-on, ignored/non-action, deferred, rejected, duplicate, accepted-risk,
   unknown, or resolved semantics, with rationale and evidence limits.
4. Resolution and non-action include explicit reopen conditions. Relevant plan,
   evidence, contract, or repository changes make stale closure fail visible.
5. Semantic equivalence, refinement, regression, split, and reopen decisions
   are model judgments preserved as append-only reconciliation events.
6. Deterministic machinery proves custody, completeness, freshness,
   idempotency, schema/version compatibility, and exact input/output hashes.
7. The evaluator selects domain critics. Each history-aware pass receives all
   relevant findings—including non-blocking and cross-domain findings—within a
   declared budget and with explicit overflow behavior.
8. Blind discovery is optional; history-aware reconciliation is mandatory
   before revise/gate consumption.
9. `no_additional_findings`, `no_open_blocking_findings`, `no_known_findings`,
   and `no_adjacent_text_matches` remain distinct claims.
10. The reviser records one action or explicit non-action per requested finding;
    the gate sees complete cumulative truth and cannot infer absence from an
    omitted prompt row.
11. WBC owns supported-runtime attempt/effect evidence, durable payload
    references, receipts, persistence, and compatibility boundaries. The
    critique ledger owns immutable critic occurrences, semantic finding
    identities, disposition/reopen events, bounded history briefings, and
    rebuildable derived projections.
12. Existing Megaplan components retain critic selection, revision, gate, and
    lifecycle authority. The migration switches the complete critique loop at
    one coordinated cutover and then retires the replaced path; custody failure
    stops admission rather than falling back silently or declaring convergence.

## Success measures

- Zero dropped flagged producer outputs and 100% reconciliation coverage.
- The M6 blocked-handoff family reconstructs as one finding with five retained
  occurrences while the accepted replay limitation retains its disposition.
- At least 50% fewer duplicate revision actions than control.
- No more than a five-percentage-point loss in independent new-family recall.
- At least 95% of closure/non-action dispositions cite adequate evidence.
- Stale briefings, missing occurrences, invalid schemas, unsupported closures,
  and incomplete WBC custody fail closed before revise/gate behavior changes.
- Minimum cutover evidence is durable and attributable to exact source,
  implementation, corpus, schema, model, and profile revisions.
- After cutover there is one supported critique-ledger architecture, no active
  legacy consumption path, and projections rebuild from WBC-backed evidence.

## Anti-scope

- No embedding database, general semantic graph, project-memory platform, or
  hard-coded similarity threshold as semantic authority.
- No parallel execution ledger, authority plane, transition writer, repair
  queue, or lifecycle state.
- No silent truncation, historical backfill that invents semantic relations,
  automatic closure from wording similarity, or severity/disposition collapse.
- No canary, prolonged shadow authority, dual-write period, broad mixed-version
  support, per-boundary rollback, or rollout dashboard program.
- No deployment, restart, chain launch, or runtime mutation by these planning
  assets themselves.
```

        ### Test Expectations
        - [fail_to_pass] test_cl1_handoff: Validates accepted_for_cl2 derivation, schema hash freshness, and blocker inventory. Currently fails for CL2 consumption (accepted_for_cl2: false, 5 blockers). CL2 must track this as a conditional-acceptance gate—all must-gates pass, schemas are frozen, but formal acceptance is pending.

- [pass_to_pass] test_semantic_loop: Validates deterministic replay: five occurrences reconcile to one semantic finding, replay twice produces identical hashes, zero-write assertion holds. Must pass—proves the replay contract CL2 must preserve when moving from in-memory to store-backed replay.
- [pass_to_pass] test_semantic_loop_failures: Validates closed-failure behavior: missing custody, invalid schema versions, corrupt evidence all fail cleanly without partial writes. CL2 must extend these to cover store-backed failure modes (PostTerminalAppendError, MonotonicSequenceError, persistence_failed).
- [pass_to_pass] test_policy_tables: Validates atomicity/failure table and privacy/retention table against landed WBC policy. Must pass—these tables define the persistence contract CL2 implements.
- [pass_to_pass] test_zero_write_mutation_gate: Validates semantic loop functions are pure—zero filesystem writes, zero subprocess calls. Must pass—CL2 replay must match this purity constraint (no model calls, no external effects).

        ### Constraints
        - Consume only the frozen CL1 handoff schemas and hashes—do not widen schema or ownership without a reviewed CL1 decision. This means routing critique domain records through existing AttemptEventType values (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, RECONCILIATION, EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME) via LedgerEvent.payload, not extending the enum.
- All writes are idempotent, content-safe, access-controlled, and fault-tested. The 4 Step 4 invariants (monotonic sequence, idempotency-key dedup, exactly-one-terminal, post-terminal rejection) are already enforced by _append_tx—CL2 must not bypass them.
- Start persistence precedes critic dispatch; terminal persistence failure is visible persistence_failed|indeterminate, never clean success. The failure-atomicity-table governs all 13 failure modes.
- Occurrences are never merged away. Semantic identity and occurrence identity remain distinct. Projections are rebuildable and carry no positive authority.
- Historical gaps stay explicit; migration never invents duplicate, resolution, evidence, or reopen relationships. LegacyClassification enforces forward-fix-only.
- Replay issues no model call or external effect. The semantic loop's zero-write assertion (verified by test_zero_write_mutation_gate) must hold for store-backed replay.
- faults.json must remain writable by critique and gate phases during CL2. The coordinated cutover to read-only projection is CL5 scope. Custody artifacts are dual-written during CL2.
- No new database service, event bus, authority plane, generalized knowledge store, embeddings, broad historical backfill, dual-write window, or old-reader retirement before CL5.
- Every accepted producer occurrence appears once; duplicate replay is a no-op; partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete ledger revision. The idempotency-key dedup (checked before rejection) and post-terminal rejection enforce this.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Phase 1—Bridge critique domain to WBC event stream: Build a CritiqueLedgerSerializer that converts CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope → LedgerEvent (event_type=EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT, payload=serialized envelope), FindingReconciliationEvent → LedgerEvent (event_type=RECONCILIATION, payload=serialized event), FindingDispositionEvent → LedgerEvent (event_type=EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME, payload=serialized event). Construct DurableRef instances for evidence payloads per the retention-class-mapping (occurrence envelopes: confidential/run; reconciliation/disposition: restricted/audit; manifests: restricted/audit). Wire through SqliteLedgerOutbox.append_event_with_outbox for atomic event+payload persistence. No enum extension needed—payload carries the typed critique record.

Phase 2—Implement occurrence append with start-persistence gate: Before critic dispatch, call _append_tx with a STARTED event. On terminal persistence failure, emit PERSISTENCE_FAILED (not clean success). Append each accepted occurrence as an EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT event with the serialized CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope in payload. Append reconciliation and disposition events after complete occurrence accounting validates. Only after all events for a revision are appended, construct and append a LedgerRevisionManifest event.

Phase 3—Cross-attempt LedgerRevisionManifest: Implement a revision_manifests table (revision_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY, prior_revision_hash TEXT, input_set_hash TEXT, domain_completeness TEXT, wbc_receipt_refs TEXT, event_ids TEXT, created_at_ns INTEGER) in the same SQLite database. After all occurrences/reconciliations/dispositions for a revision are atomically appended, INSERT the manifest in the same transaction. This avoids widening attempt_events.

Phase 4—Replay indexes: Create a round_attempts mapping table (round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)) populated at occurrence-write time from CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label. Add CREATE INDEX idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence) for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans. Per-attempt replay uses existing PRIMARY KEY; domain/round replay joins round_attempts → attempt_events; cumulative rebuild scans idx_attempt_events_sequence.

Phase 5—One-time M6 import: Traverse m6-corpus.json rounds v1-v5. For each round: map critique_custody.findings[] to CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope instances, evaluator_verdict.flag_verifications[] to FindingReconciliationEvent/DispositionEvent instances, plan-level faults/contract to LedgerRevisionManifest inputs. Classify every legacy record through LegacyClassification: has_authoritative_source → BACKFILLED; otherwise UNKNOWN or UNRECONSTRUCTABLE. Apply the partial-parse contract from failure-atomicity-table even though the corpus has zero failures. Reject unsupported schema versions via SUPPORTED_VERSIONS gate before any write. Import into the WBC store using the same atomic append path—each imported occurrence is an immutable event. Mark all imported records with metadata.source='m6-corpus-one-time-import'.

Phase 6—Faults.json dual-write: Keep save_flag_registry writing to faults.json unchanged. Add a parallel WBC write: after update_flags_after_critique upserts FlagRecords into the registry, also emit a critique-ledger flag-sync event (EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT with a FlagSyncEnvelope payload) into the WBC store. This dual-writes faults.json (for active readers during CL2) and the ledger (for eventual cutover to read-only projection in CL5).

Phase 7—Fixtures and negative tests: Add all required fixtures (current-source import, target-only, future-version, corrupt, redacted, unavailable, duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, crash). Prove negative tests: ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue, Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state. Prove duplicate replay is a no-op via idempotency-key dedup. Prove partial publication cannot expose incomplete revision (post-terminal rejection + atomic outbox).

- [assume_and_proceed] Domain-bounded and round-scoped replay have no existing index support in the WBC store—domain and round_label exist only in CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope (event_json), not as columns in attempt_events. The constraint forbids widening the attempt_events schema. What is the minimum viable approach within the two-week scope? _(assumption: Create a round_attempts mapping table (round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (round_label, attempt_id)) populated at occurrence-write time from CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label. Domain-bounded replay extends this pattern with a domain_attempts table if needed, or filters in memory after loading round-scoped events since domain cardinality is bounded (2-4 domains per briefing budget). Add CREATE INDEX idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence) for efficient cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans. This avoids widening attempt_events while enabling all four required replay patterns within the two-week scope.)_

        Idea:
# CL2 — WBC-backed ledger persistence, import, and replay

## Outcome

Implement the CL1 record contract through existing WBC/plan-local persistence
seams, with atomic append, idempotent retry, rebuildable projections, read-only
replay, and one-time import from retained legacy artifacts. Leave CL3 one stable
target API and fixtures.

## In scope

- Persist critic start/result and immutable occurrence envelopes with WBC
  attempt/payload references and current raw/custody artifacts.
- Append reconciliation/disposition events and publish a ledger revision only
  after complete occurrence accounting validates.
- Build cumulative/domain projections and deterministic read-only replay.
- Add current-source import, target-only, future-version, corrupt, redacted,
  unavailable, duplicate, out-of-order, concurrent, and crash fixtures.
- Add the target schema reader and explicit `legacy_unknown` import behavior;
  retain current flag/gate/finalize inputs only until the coordinated cutover.
- Implement freshness vectors and governed tombstone/unavailable handling.

## Out of scope

Evaluator selection, critic prompt changes, semantic auto-merge policy, reviser
or gate consumption, live cutover, old artifact deletion, or production
backfill of historical semantic relationships.

## Locked decisions

- Start persistence precedes critic dispatch; terminal persistence failure is
  visible `persistence_failed|indeterminate`, never clean success.
- Occurrences are never merged away. Semantic identity and occurrence identity
  remain distinct.
- Projections are rebuildable and carry no positive authority.
- Historical gaps stay explicit; migration never invents duplicate, resolution,
  evidence, or reopen relationships.
- WBC inline/reference, retention, redaction, ordering, and effect rules are
  reused, not reimplemented.

## Open questions

- Which landed backend satisfies one transaction, and where is durable
  prepare/outbox reconciliation required?
- What plan-local compatibility files remain necessary for old readers?
- How are partially parseable failed attempts admitted without promoting an
  invalid whole payload?
- What indexes are necessary within the two-week scope for domain/round replay?

## Constraints

Consume only an accepted CL1 handoff. Do not widen schema or ownership without a
new reviewed CL1 decision. All writes are idempotent, content-safe, access-
controlled, and fault-tested. Replay issues no model call or external effect.

## Done criteria

- Schema/golden, property, concurrency, crash/fault, compatibility, privacy,
  WBC ledger/evidence, and existing critique-custody suites pass.
- Every accepted producer occurrence appears once; duplicate replay is a no-op;
  partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete ledger revision.
- Projections rebuild byte-equivalently from retained inputs and surface
  missing/redacted evidence as unknown.
- The one-time importer preserves all available legacy evidence, marks semantic
  gaps unknown, and rejects unsupported target versions without partial publish.
- Negative tests prove ledger/replay cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue,
  Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state.

## Touchpoints

`arnold/workflow/execution_attempt_ledger.py`, `payload_policy.py`, boundary
evidence/compatibility/conformance; Megaplan critique custody/runtime, schemas,
artifact writers, flag registry readers, and focused WBC/critique tests.

## Anti-scope

No new database service, event bus, authority plane, generalized knowledge
store, embeddings, broad historical backfill, dual-write window, or old-reader
retirement before CL5.

## Written handoff to CL3

Write and review `docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json` with API/
schema hashes, backend/atomicity decision, one-time import map, replay hashes,
fixture index, freshness rules, fault results, backup/restore prerequisites, and
unresolved limits. CL3 must validate the handoff and replay fixtures before
constructing a briefing.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - Size each step so it can be implemented AND verified in a single worker turn (~15 min, one model conversation). A step that bundles "create the abstraction AND migrate every consumer AND write all the tests" is too large to finish in one turn and will fail (the worker runs out of time before it can implement, leaving the step blocked). For large mechanical refactors, split along the natural seam — one step to add the new abstraction + its unit tests, then one step per consumer to migrate (the last migration step also adds parity tests) — rather than one giant step. Higher complexity does not earn a step more time; it only routes to a stronger model for the same single turn.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.
        - Assign every plan step a complexity score 1–5 before finalize. Reason about complexity in the plan markdown (e.g. "Complexity: 2" per step), and surface it informally so the reviewer can audit:
          - 1 = trivial single-file mechanical change
          - 2 = simple change with tests to update
          - 3 = multi-file change with non-trivial logic
          - 4 = cross-cutting change with architecture implications
          - 5 = fundamental system change with high regression risk
        - Populate `changed_surfaces` with every concrete file path your plan will change or create. Include both source files and test files. Use repo-relative paths (e.g., `src/prompts.py`, `tests/test_prompts.py`). This list drives the deterministic test-selection blast radius; be complete. If your plan touches a file, list it.
        - (Optional) Populate `test_blast_radius` with your own scoped test-selection proposal. This complements the deterministic floor the system computes from `changed_surfaces`; you cannot narrow below the floor, but you can widen with additional selectors or escalate to full. If you intend a scoped finalize baseline while keeping the full suite as a hard gate, set `strategy` to "scoped", include concrete `selectors`, and set `full_suite_fallback` to true; the floor's full-suite requirement will be honored by the fallback without forcing the baseline strategy to "full".
        - When you include `test_blast_radius`, it MUST be schema-complete. Include every
field in this shape:
`{"strategy":"scoped","confidence":"high","selectors":[{"kind":"path","value":"tests/test_relevant.py","reason":"covers the changed surface"}],"changed_surfaces":["src/relevant.py"],"always_run":[],"full_suite_fallback":true,"rationale":"Why these tests cover the planned changes.","import_graph":{"degraded":false,"dependent_tests":0,"unresolved":[]}}`.
The runtime deterministically recomputes and merges the import graph and the
coverage floor from `changed_surfaces`; do not omit `confidence`, `always_run`,
`changed_surfaces`, or `import_graph` merely because those values are also
runtime-derived.

````md

...

...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

### [msg 141] role=tool ts=2026-07-24 04:38:45 UTC content=9175b tool_call_id=call_-7412290121766005373
{"content": "     1|\"\"\"Durable SQLite-backed store for ``ExecutionAttemptLedger`` event streams.\n     2|\n     3|This module provides the M6A transactional store boundary for WBC ledger\n     4|events. It does NOT modify the ``ExecutionAttemptLedger`` schema — it reads\n     5|and writes the same frozen dataclasses via their existing ``to_dict()``\n     6|serialization contract.\n     7|\n     8|Key invariants:\n     9|* SQLite WAL mode for concurrent readers and atomic writes.\n    10|* Contract-version binding to ``LEDGER_SCHEMA_VERSION`` in metadata.\n    11|* Durable serialization uses ``LedgerEvent.to_dict()`` and json.\n    12|* Readback reconstructs ``LedgerEvent`` and ``ExecutionAttemptLedger``\n    13|  without mutating schema fields.\n    14|\n    15|Step 4 transactional append invariants (enforced inside ONE SQLite\n    16|``BEGIN IMMEDIATE`` transaction per append):\n    17|\n    18|* **Monotonic sequence** — an appended event's ``sequence`` must be\n    19|  strictly greater than the largest persisted sequence for the same\n    20|  ``attempt_id``. A regression raises :class:`MonotonicSequenceError`.\n    21|* **Idempotency-key uniqueness with dedup** — appending an event whose\n    22|  ``(attempt_id, idempotency_key)`` already exists does not raise; the\n    23|  store returns the existing persisted event with ``is_duplicate=True``.\n    24|  Two different events with the same idempotency key can never coexist.\n    25|* **Exactly one terminal event** — once a terminal event\n    26|  (``completed``/``failed``/``cancelled``) is persisted for an attempt,\n    27|  any further append with a new idempotency key raises\n    28|  :class:`PostTerminalAppendError`. A second terminal therefore cannot\n    29|  land, and neither can any post-terminal non-terminal event.\n    30|* **Dedup wins over rejection** — when a duplicate idempotency key is\n    31|  presented, the existing event is returned even if the attempt has\n    32|  since reached a terminal state.
