### [msg 158] role=tool ts=2026-07-24 04:38:45 UTC content=4890b tool_call_id=call_-7412257411295082765
{"content": "     1|\"\"\"Versioned, checksum-validated, crash-resumable schema migrations for the\n     2|M6A WBC transactional ledger substrate.\n     3|\n     4|This module provides the M6A migration boundary. It governs how the SQLite\n     5|schema that backs :class:`~arnold.workflow.attempt_ledger_store.SqliteAttemptLedgerStore`\n     6|and its sibling stores (outbox, payload) evolves over time, and how legacy or\n     7|unreconstructable records are classified.\n     8|\n     9|Design pillars (M6A success criterion 9):\n    10|* **Versioned** — every migration carries a strictly-increasing integer\n    11|  ``version`` and a stable ``name``. Migrations apply in ascending order and a\n    12|  gap in the applied sequence is never bridged silently.\n    13|* **Checksum-validated** — every migration's content (version + name +\n    14|  statements) is reduced to a deterministic SHA-256 checksum that is persisted\n    15|  alongside its applied-state record. On re-open the stored checksum is\n    16|  compared to the live registry's checksum; a mismatch is a forward-fix\n    17|  violation and is surfaced, never silently re-applied.\n    18|* **Crash-resumable** — each migration applies inside its *own* ``BEGIN\n    19|  IMMEDIATE`` transaction that atomically stages the schema change *and* the\n    20|  ``schema_migrations`` row. A crash mid-migration leaves either a fully\n    21|  applied+recorded migration or nothing — never a half-applied migration that a\n    22|  mixed-version reader could mistake for authoritative state. Resuming re-runs\n    23|  the unrecorded migration from scratch; DDL is idempotent (``IF NOT EXISTS``)\n    24|  so a partially-staged-but-uncommitted change is safely re-staged.\n    25|* **Forward-fix only** — there is no downgrade path. A migration, once applied,\n    26|  is immutable. To repair a defect you add a *new* migration; you never edit an\n    27|  applied one (the checksum guard enforces this). Unreconstructable legacy\n    28|  records are classified ``UNKNOWN`` / ``UNRECONSTRUCTABLE`` — success is never\n    29|  synthesized from logs, markers, or mutable state (SD2).\n    30|* **Idempotent on duplicate backfill** — ``migrate()`` is safe to call any\n    31|  number of times; already-applied migrations are skipped, not re-executed.\n    32|* **Mixed reader/writer safe** — migration state is a normal table read under\n    33|  WAL snapshot semantics, so a reader connection observes a consistent\n    34|  ``schema_migrations`` view (some prefix ``1..k`` applied) even while a writer\n    35|  is mid-migration.\n    36|\n    37|This module owns only migration-tracking and legacy-classification tables. It\n    38|does not duplicate the store/outbox/payload DDL; forward migrations are\n    39|additive and idempotent.\n    40|\"\"\"\n    41|\n    42|from __future__ import annotations\n    43|\n    44|import hashlib\n    45|import sqlite3\n    46|import time\n    47|import uuid\n    48|from abc import ABC, abstractmethod\n    49|from dataclasses import dataclass, field\n    50|from enum import Enum\n    51|from typing import Any, Callable, Optional, Sequence\n    52|\n    53|from arnold.workflow.attempt_ledger_store import SqliteAttemptLedgerStore\n    54|\n    55|# ── Constants ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    56|\n    57|#: Logical tag embedded in every migration checksum so that checksums from a\n    58|#: different (hypothetical) migration scheme never collide with ours.\n    59|_CHECKSUM_DOMAIN: str = \"arnold.workflow.ledger_migrations.v1\"\n    60|\n    61|#: Number of times to retry ``BEGIN IMMEDIATE`` under ``SQLITE_BUSY`` before\n    62|#: giving up. Mirrors the store/outbox contention handling so a migration run\n    63|#: is safe under separate-process writer contention.\n    64|_BEGIN_RETRY_ATTEMPTS: int = 30\n    65|_BEGIN_RETRY_BASE_DELAY: float = 0.05  # 50 ms\n    66|\n    67|# ── Migration-tracking table DDL ──────────────────────────────────────────\n    68|#\n    69|# The migration-tracking tables are *bootstrap* state: they are created with\n    70|# ``IF NOT EXISTS`` before any versioned query so that ``get_state()`` works on\n    71|# a brand-new store. They are NOT themselves a versioned migration — versioned\n    72|# migrations are additive schema evolution layered on top.\n    73|\n    74|_SCHEMA_MIGRATIONS_TABLE_DDL: str = \"\"\"\\\n    75|CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_migrations (\n    76|    version       INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,\n    77|    name          TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    78|    checksum      TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    79|    applied_at_ns INTEGER NOT NULL,\n    80|    status        TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT 'applied'\n    81|", "total_lines": 1212, "file_size": 49490, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=81 to continue reading (showing 1-80 of 1212 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

These target different things: the epic anti-scope is about running two active *critique-loop architectures* in parallel (invariant 12: one coordinated cutover then retire); the CL2 custody dual-write is keeping existing plan-local artifacts writable *while* the ledger is being built, so current phases keep working. To respect both, the ledger persistence/replay/import/handoff work is unambiguous `must` scope; the `faults.json` flag-sync emission (Step 8) is a strictly-additive `should` that never alters `faults.json` write behavior, and it can be deferred if a reviewer reads the epic anti-scope as also covering custody artifacts. No narrowing of the local brief; the brief is implemented in full, with the dual-write item isolated as the only tension.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 1: Foundation — pure serializer + companion tables\\n\\n### Step 1: Critique↔WBC record serializer (`arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_bridge.py`)\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 3** (multi-field mapping with `LedgerEvent` identity scaffolding; round-trip fidelity)\\n\\nBuild a pure, side-effect-free serializer that maps the five frozen CL1 record types to/from `LedgerEvent.payload` using existing generic event types — no enum extension.\\n\\n1. **Define a discriminator-tagged payload envelope.** Every critique record serialized into `LedgerEvent.payload` carries `{\\\"cl_record_type\\\": <tag>, \\\"schema_version\\\": \\\"cl.schema.v1\\\", \\\"record\\\": <record.to_dict()>}` so deserialization reconstructs the exact typed dataclass by inspecting `cl_record_type`. Tags: `occurrence_envelope`, `reconciliation_event`, `disposition_event`, `revision_manifest`, `flag_sync`.\\n2. **Map record → event type (fixed, from locked decision):**\\n   - `CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope` → `AttemptEventType.EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT`\\n   - `FindingReconciliationEvent` → `AttemptEventType.RECONCILIATION`\\n   - `FindingDispositionEvent` → `AttemptEventType.EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME`\\n   - `LedgerRevisionManifest` → `AttemptEventType.EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` (the cross-attempt companion-table write is Step 4)\\n3. **Add an identity-scaffolding factory** `build_critique_event_identity(occurrence|attempt_id, run_id, graph_revision)` that returns a minimal valid `AttemptIdentity` (note `attempt_id` must be a valid UUID — `execution_attempt_ledger.py:456-462`), plus `AttemptProvenance`, `RuntimeAdapter(AdapterKind.MEGAPLAN_AUDITOR, ...)`, `VersionSet`, `GrantRef`. Keep the factory parameterized so callers supply provenance; never invent lineage.\\n4. **Implement** `serialize_occurrence/serialize_reconciliation/serialize_disposition/serialize_manifest → LedgerEvent` and a single `deserialize_event(LedgerEvent) → record|None` dispatching on `payload.cl_record_type`. Use the records' own `canonical_hash`/`occurrence_id`/`disposition_id` to derive stable idempotency keys (e.g. `f\\\"cl2:occ:{attempt_id}:{occurrence_id}\\\"`) so retries dedup correctly.\\n5. **Tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_ledger_bridge.py`): round-trip byte-identical `to_dict()` equality for all five record types; unsupported `schema_version` raises `ValueError` (parity with `SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` gate); unknown `cl_record_type` deserializes to `None` without raising; idempotency key is stable across re-serialization of the same record.\\n\\n### Step 2: Critique-ledger companion tables (`arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_tables.py`)\\n**Scope:** Small · **Complexity: 2** (DDL + idempotent init on shared connection)\\n\\nAdd the cross-attempt aggregation/replay companion tables on the same SQLite connection, mirroring how `SqliteLedgerPayloadStore._init_schema` (`ledger_payload_store.py:705`) self-initializes on `store.conn`. Do **not** touch the frozen `attempt_events` DDL or the migration registry.\\n\\n1. **`CritiqueLedgerTables(store)` with `ensure_tables()`** running one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (idempotent `IF NOT EXISTS`):\\n   - `revision_manifests(revision_hash TEXT PK, prior_revision_hash TEXT, input_set_hash TEXT, domain_completeness TEXT, wbc_receipt_refs TEXT, event_ids TEXT, manifest_json TEXT, created_at_ns INTEGER)`\\n   - `round_attempts(round_label TEXT, attempt_id TEXT, PRIMARY KEY(round_label, attempt_id))` — populated at occurrence-write time from `CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope.round_label`\\n   - `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_attempt_events_sequence ON attempt_events(sequence)` for cumulative cross-attempt ordered scans\\n2. **Tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_ledger_tables.py`): `ensure_tables()` is idempotent on a temp `SqliteAttemptLedgerStore`; tables/index exist after init; re-run is a no-op (no error, no duplicate index).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 2: Write path — atomic append with start-persistence gate\\n\\n### Step 3: CritiqueLedgerWriter — occurrence/reconciliation/disposition append + start gate (`arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_writer.py`)\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 4** (atomic append via outbox + payload store, start-persistence gate, four invariants, terminal-failure visibility)\\n\\nThe writer orchestrates the atomic append path through `SqliteLedgerOutbox.append_event_with_outbox` + `SqliteLedgerPayloadStore.store_payload`, reusing all existing enforcement.\\n\\n1. **Start-persistence gate.** Before critic dispatch, call `store.append_started(attempt_id, started_event)` (`attempt_ledger_store.py:1171`). On `PersistenceStatus.PERSISTENCE_FAILED`/`INDETERMINATE` (or an exception), append a `PERSISTENCE_FAILED` event and surface the failure — **never** proceed to occurrence write (failure-atomicity-table modes `start_persistence_failed`/`start_persistence_indeterminate`). No `CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope` is created on a failed start.\\n2. **Occurrence append.** For each accepted occurrence: `store_payload(occurrence.to_dict(), privacy_class=CONFIDENTIAL, retention derived from retention-class-mapping)` → `DurableRef`; set `evidence_ref`; build the `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` event via the Step 1 serializer with the `DurableRef`/tagged dict in `payload`; append through `append_event_with_outbox` so event + outbox land atomically. Also `INSERT` into `round_attempts` in the same transaction (open one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` around the outbox call's transaction boundary via the shared `store.conn` — see implementation note below).\\n3. **Reconciliation/disposition append.** After *complete occurrence accounting validates* (all parseable occurrences have a reconciliation; all findings have a disposition), append `RECONCILIATION` and `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_OUTCOME` events. Reuse `replay_full`'s coverage checks (`semantic_loop.py:1115-1159`) as the gate before these writes.\\n4. **Idempotent retry + dedup.** Duplicate occurrence (same idempotency key) returns `is_duplicate=True` and is a no-op (dedup wins over rejection — `ledger_outbox.py:380-393`). Duplicate replay of a whole revision therefore cannot double-count.\\n5. **Tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_ledger_writer.py`): positive append round-trips through `read_events`; start-persistence failure emits `PERSISTENCE_FAILED` and writes no occurrence; duplicate occurrence is a no-op; post-terminal append rejects with `PostTerminalAppendError`; monotonic violation raises `MonotonicSequenceError`.\\n\\n> **Implementation note for the worker:** `append_event_with_outbox` opens/closes its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. To keep `round_attempts` insertion in the same atomic boundary, perform the occurrence append in one `store.conn` transaction using the same `_begin_immediate_retry(conn)` helper pattern as `ledger_outbox.py:369` (INSERT event + INSERT round_attempts + INSERT outbox), or add an outbox-style method on `SqliteLedgerOutbox` that accepts extra same-transaction DDL. Prefer the smallest change that keeps event + mapping atomic; do **not** widen `attempt_events`.\\n\\n### Step 4: Cross-attempt LedgerRevisionManifest publish (`arnold/critique_ledger/ledger_writer.py`, extend Step 3 module)\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 3** (cross-attempt companion-table INSERT atomic with the manifest event)\\n\\n`LedgerRevisionManifest` spans multiple attempts; the store is attempt-scoped, so the manifest lands in `revision_manifests`.\\n\\n1. **Publish gate.** Only after *all* occurrence/reconciliation/disposition events for a revision validate (re-run the semantic-loop custody+reconciliation+disposition coverage checks) construct the `LedgerRevisionManifest` (use `construct_manifest`, `semantic_loop.py:1162`) with `input_set_hash`, `prior_revision_hash`, `domain_completeness`, `wbc_receipt_refs`, `event_ids`.\\n2. **Atomic publish.** Append the manifest event (Step 1 serializer, `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT`) and `INSERT INTO revision_manifests` in the **same** `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction on `store.conn`. An incomplete revision produces **no** `revision_manifests` row — partial publication cannot expose an accepted incomplete manifest.\\n3. **Chain integrity.** `prior_revision_hash` must equal the previous manifest's `revision_hash` or be `None` for revision 1; a mismatch is a hard failure (fail closed, no manifest written).\\n4. **Tests** (extend `test_ledger_writer.py`): manifest appears only after full coverage; simulating incomplete reconciliation leaves `revision_manifests` empty; manifest row's `event_ids` exactly equals the appended event idempotency keys; `prior_revision_hash` mismatch raises and writes nothing.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 3: Read path — store-backed deterministic replay\\n\\n### Step 5: Store-backed replay reader, four patterns (`arnold/critique_ledger/store_replay.py`)\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 3** (four read patterns, zero-write, byte-equivalence to in-memory)\\n\\nA read-only reader that loads `attempt_events`, deserializes payloads via Step 1, and feeds the existing pure `replay_full`. It must issue **zero** filesystem/subprocess writes (parity with `test_zero_write_mutation_gate`).\\n\\n1. **Four replay patterns:**\\n   - **(a) per-attempt** — `store.read_events(attempt_id)` (existing PK scan, `attempt_ledger_store.py:1199`).\\n   - **(b) round-scoped** — join `round_attempts` → `attempt_events` for a `round_label`.\\n   - **(c) domain-bounded** — load round-scoped events then filter in memory by domain (domain cardinality is bounded 2–4 per `BRIEFING_BUDGETS`; no `domain_attempts` table needed).\\n   - **(d) cumulative** — scan `idx_attempt_events_sequence` across all attempts in append order for full projection rebuild.\\n2. **Deserializer pipeline:** `events → deserialize_event → (occurrences, reconciliations, dispositions) → replay_full(...)`. Reuse `replay_full` unchanged.\\n3. **Determinism/purity:** replay twice → identical projection hashes; produces no writes (assert against a write-detector harness). Missing/redacted evidence surfaces as `UNKNOWN` (parity with `EvidenceAvailability.UNAVAILABLE` + reopen condition).\\n4. **Tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_store_replay.py`): write a revision via Step 3/4 then (a)–(d) replay yields byte-identical projections to the in-memory `replay_full` baseline; two replays produce identical hashes; replay against a partially-populated store (no manifest) raises a typed failure, never silently truncates; a write-detector context manager proves zero writes/subprocess calls during replay.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 4: Import + compatibility\\n\\n### Step 6: One-time M6 corpus importer (`arnold/critique_ledger/m6_importer.py`)\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 4** (corpus traversal, forward-fix classification, atomic import, schema rejection)\\n\\nImport the 5 retained M6 rounds into the CL1 schema target exactly once, never inventing semantic relationships.\\n\\n1. **Validate handoff + corpus freshness first.** Recompute the recorded hashes from `cl1-contract-oracle.json` (same `HASHED_PATHS` approach as `tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl1_handoff.py:25`); reject before any write if schemas are stale or unsupported (`SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` gate).\\n2. **Traverse** `m6-corpus.json` rounds v1–v5: map each round's `critique_custody.findings[]` → `CritiqueOccurrenceEnvelope` (occurrence_id = `finding_id`, finding_id preserved, `round_label` from the round), `evaluator_verdict.flag_verifications[]` → `FindingReconciliationEvent`/`FindingDispositionEvent`, plan-level faults/contract → `LedgerRevisionManifest` inputs.\\n3. **Classify every legacy record** through `LegacyClassification.classify(ClassificationEvidence(...))` (`ledger_migrations.py:620`): only `has_authoritative_source=True` (the corpus is the authoritative retained source) permits `BACKFILLED`; anything ambiguous → `UNKNOWN`. Apply the failure-atomicity-table partial-parse contract even though the corpus has zero failures (so the importer is correct if rerun on a non-clean corpus).\\n4. **Import atomically** via the Step 3 writer path — each occurrence is one immutable event. Mark every imported record `metadata.source='m6-corpus-one-time-import'` and record its `LegacyClassification`. Idempotent: re-running against an already-imported revision is a no-op (dedup by idempotency key).\\n5. **Reject unsupported target versions** without partial publish (all-or-nothing per round).\\n6. **Tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_m6_importer.py`): import all 5 rounds into a fresh store; occurrence count matches corpus finding counts; replay of the imported store matches a golden projection; re-import is a no-op (event counts unchanged); a synthesized unsupported-schema corpus is rejected with zero events written; a synthesized corrupt round classifies `CORRUPT`/`UNKNOWN` and never `BACKFILLED` from circumstantial evidence.\\n\\n### Step 7: CL1 conditional-acceptance consumption — fail_to_pass (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_cl1_consumption.py`)\\n**Scope:** Small · **Complexity: 2** (test + minimal acceptance-tracking helper)\\n\\nAddress the `fail_to_pass` `test_cl1_handoff`: the handoff is formally `accepted_for_cl2: false` with 5 procedural blockers, but all must-gates pass and schemas are frozen. CL2 proceeds conditionally and tracks the blockers.\\n\\n1. **Add `validate_cl1_consumption(handoff)` helper** (in `ledger_bridge.py` or a small `cl1_consumption.py`): returns `{may_proceed: bool, must_gates_pass: bool, schemas_frozen: bool, open_blockers: [...]}`. `may_proceed` requires all must-gates pass AND all five schema hashes fresh AND blockers recorded — but does **not** require `accepted_for_cl2.value` to be `True`.\\n2. **Test** (`test_cl2_cl1_consumption.py`): against the real handoff, `may_proceed is True` while `accepted_for_cl2.value is False`; the 5 blockers are enumerated as open gates; a stale schema hash flips `may_proceed` to `False`; an altered handoff where a must-gate fails flips `may_proceed` to `False`. This is the fail_to_pass→pass.\\n\\n> This does **not** modify the frozen `cl1-contract-oracle.json` — it consumes it conditionally, matching the brief's tracked-open-gates stance.\\n\\n### Step 8: `faults.json` flag-sync dual-write (`arnold/critique_ledger/flag_sync.py`) — `should`\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 3** (additive dual-write; must not change existing `faults.json` behavior)\\n\\nAdditive compatibility bridge. **Strictly additive**: `faults.json` write behavior is unchanged; this only *additionally* emits a ledger flag-sync event. If a reviewer reads the epic \\\"no dual-write window\\\" anti-scope as also covering custody artifacts, this step is deferred to CL5 cutover prep without affecting any `must`.\\n\\n1. **`emit_flag_sync_event(writer, flag_records, plan_dir)`** builds a `FlagSyncEnvelope` (tagged payload from Step 1) and appends an `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` event into the WBC store after `update_flags_after_critique` upserts `FlagRecord`s into the registry. It is invoked from the critique-custody orchestration seam — **do not** alter `save_flag_registry`/`load_flag_registry` (`arnold_pipelines/megaplan/flags.py`).\\n2. **Tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_flag_sync.py`): after a flag upsert, a matching flag-sync event exists in the store; `faults.json` content is byte-identical with and without the hook (proving non-interference); duplicate sync is a no-op.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Phase 5: Hardening — fixtures, negative tests, handoff\\n\\n### Step 9: Fixtures + negative mutation tests\\n**Scope:** Medium · **Complexity: 3** (many fixtures + prove replay/writer cannot mutate external state)\\n\\n1. **Add fixtures** under `tests/fixtures/critique_ledger/cl2/`: `current_source.json`, `target_only.json`, `future_version.json`, `corrupt.json`, `redacted.json`, `unavailable.json`, `duplicate.json`, `out_of_order.json`, `concurrent.json`, `crash.json`. Each is a minimal, hand-verified record set exercising one failure/edge mode.\\n2. **Negative tests** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_store_replay_negatives.py`): using the `WriteDetector` harness pattern from `tests/arnold_pipelines/megaplan/test_zero_write_mutation_gate.py:63`, prove store-backed replay and the writer cannot mutate plan, gate, lifecycle, queue, Git/provider, delivery, or external-effect state. Prove: duplicate replay is a no-op; out-of-order append raises `MonotonicSequenceError`; concurrent append serializes under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; a simulated crash mid-revision leaves no `revision_manifests` row (partial publication cannot expose an incomplete revision); corrupt payload surfaces as `UNKNOWN`, never as an invented finding.\\n\\n### Step 10: CL2 handoff document + validation test (`docs/critique-ledger/handoffs/cl2-ledger-replay.json`)\\n**Scope:** Small · **Complexity: 2** (JSON handoff + structural test)\\n\\n1. **Write the handoff** with: API/schema hashes (recomputed for the new modules + frozen CL1 schemas), backend/atomicity decision (one-transaction via shared WAL conn + `append_event_with_outbox`), one-time import map (M6 rounds → event counts + classifications), replay hashes (per-pattern, two-runs-equal), fixture index, freshness rules (schema-hash recompute on open), fault results (all failure-atomicity-table modes covered), backup/restore prerequisites, and unresolved limits (open CL1 blockers, dual-write deferral note).\\n2. **Test** (`tests/arnold/critique_ledger/test_cl2_handoff.py`): handoff exists, is valid JSON, declares `cl.handoff.v1`, all recorded file hashes recompute fresh, replay hashes are self-consistent, and the open CL1 blockers are carried forward verbatim from `cl1-contract-oracle.json`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Execution Order\\n1. **Phase 1 first** — the serializer (Step 1) is the foundation every other step imports; the tables (Step 2) unblock the writer/replay.\\n2. **Phase 2** — writer + manifest publish (Steps 3–4) produce the data the read path validates.\\n3. **Phase 3** — store-backed replay (Step 5) is the deterministic-correctness proof and the verification harness for Steps 3–4 and 6.\\n4. **Phase 4** — M6 importer (Step 6) and conditional-acceptance (Step 7) are gated on the write+read paths; the `should` flag-sync (Step 8) is last because it is deferrable.\\n5. **Phase 5** — fixtures + negatives (Step 9) harden everything; the handoff (Step 10) is the final deliverable, written only after all `must` tests pass.\\n\\n## Validation Order\\n1. Run the new unit tests per step (`run_tests`), cheapest first: Step 1 → Step 2 → Step 5.\\n2. After the write path lands: Step 3/4 tests + the existing pass_to_pass suites `test_attempt_ledger_store`, `test_ledger_outbox`, `test_ledger_payload_enforcement`.\\n3. After replay lands: Step 5 determinism + `test_semantic_loop`, `test_semantic_loop_failures`, `test_zero_write_mutation_gate` must all pass.\\n4. After import: Step 6 + `test_m6_corpus`, `test_cl1_handoff`, `test_policy_tables`.\\n5. Negative tests (Step 9) and the handoff freshness test (Step 10) run last as the full-suite gate.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Success Criteria\\n\\n- {\\\\\\\"criterion\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"CritiqueLedgerSerializer round-trips all five frozen CL1 record types through LedgerEvent.payload and reconstructs them byte-identically (to_dict equality); all `must` criteria are independent of it.)\\n2. **Manifest event type.** I route `LedgerRevisionManifest` through `EXTERNAL_EFFECT_INTENT` (payload-carried) plus the companion `revision_manifests` row. Is there a reviewer preference to instead reuse `RECONCILIATION` for the manifest (it is a cross-attempt aggregation)? Both avoid enum extension; the choice affects deserialization tagging only.\\n\\n## Assumptions\\n- The new persistence I/O modules live under `arnold/critique_ledger/` as separate submodules (`ledger_bridge`, `ledger_tables`, `ledger_writer`, `store_replay`, `m6_importer`, `flag_sync`) and are **not** re-exported from the pure `__init__.py`, preserving the package-root zero-write contract; consumers import them directly. Only the pure serializer helpers may be added to `__init__` if desired.\\n- `revision_manifests`/`round_attempts`/`idx_attempt_events_sequence` are created on `store.conn` via `CritiqueLedgerTables.ensure_tables()` (additive, idempotent), not via the frozen `attempt_events` DDL and not via the migration registry (which would require checksum management).\\n- Domain-bounded replay uses in-memory filtering after round-scoped load (domain cardinality bounded 2–4 by `BRIEFING_BUDGETS`), so no `domain_attempts` table is needed.\\n- The `fail_to_pass` `test_cl1_handoff` is satisfied by adding a CL2-side conditional-acceptance consumption test + helper (Step 7), not by altering the frozen `cl1-contract-oracle.json`. The frozen handoff's `accepted_for_cl2.value=False` (correctly derived) is left intact.\\n- Tests use temp/in-memory SQLite stores, matching the existing `test_attempt_ledger_store.py` pattern.\\n- The `external_effect_intent` payload-or-policy requirement (`execution_attempt_ledger.py:837`) is satisfied because every occurrence/manifest event carries a tagged dict or `DurableRef` ### [msg 188] role=assistant ts=2026-07-24 04:38:45 UTC content=2859b finish=stop
I've written the implementation plan. Here's a summary of what I built and the key decisions.

## Plan: CL2 — WBC-backed ledger persistence, import, and replay

**Verified against the codebase** before committing: the WBC substrate already provides one-transaction atomicity (shared SQLite WAL conn + `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` in `_append_tx` / `append_event_with_outbox`), `LedgerEvent.payload` accepts arbitrary `dict|DurableRef`, and the five CL1 schemas are frozen at `cl.schema.v1`. So the brief's "bridge through payload, no enum extension" approach is the correct and minimal path — I adopted it as the starting hypothesis rather than inventing a parallel store.

### Structure — 5 phases, 10 worker-sized steps
- **Phase 1 (Foundation):** pure serializer (`ledger_bridge.py`) that round-trips all 5 record types through `LedgerEvent.payload` with a `cl_record_type` discriminator; companion tables (`revision_manifests`, `round_attempts`, sequence index) on the shared connection — *not* widening `attempt_events`.
- **Phase 2 (Write path):** `CritiqueLedgerWriter` with the start-persistence gate (STARTED before dispatch, `PERSISTENCE_FAILED` on terminal failure), occurrence/reconciliation/disposition append via `append_event_with_outbox` + `store_payload`, and cross-attempt manifest publish that's atomic with its final event.
- **Phase 3 (Read path):** store-backed replay covering all 4 patterns (per-attempt, round, domain-filter-in-memory, cumulative), feeding the existing pure `replay_full` — zero-write.
- **Phase 4 (Import/compat):** M6 one-time importer using `LegacyClassification.classify()` (forward-fix-only), the `fail_to_pass` conditional-acceptance test, and the strictly-additive `faults.json` flag-sync.
- **Phase 5 (Hardening):** 10 fixtures + negative mutation tests + the `cl2-ledger-replay.json` handoff.

### Two things I flagged rather than optimized around
1. **Anchor tension (surfaced explicitly):** the epic North Star anti-scope says "no dual-write window before CL5," but the CL2 brief mandates a `faults.json` ↔ ledger flag-sync. I read these as targeting different layers (critique-loop cutover vs. custody plumbing) and isolated the flag-sync as a **deferrable `should`**; all `must` criteria are independent of it. This is raised as Question 1 for the reviewer.
2. **The `fail_to_pass` `test_cl1_handoff`:** the existing test actually passes (it validates the handoff as-is). I treated the real gap as a new CL2-side conditional-acceptance consumption test (`may_proceed=True` while `accepted_for_cl2=False`, blockers tracked) that leaves the frozen handoff untouched.

Complexity ranges 2–4 per step (no 5s), every step sized for one ~15-min turn, with changed surfaces and a scoped test blast radius (full-suite fallback on) included. The full plan is at `.megaplan/plans/cl2-wbc-backed-ledger-20260724-0351/plan.md`.
